# Actuated valves and piping parts catalog

Contents: 1. Motor and hydraulic valves · 2. Solenoid and control valves · 3. Piping parts · Sources

Symbols: ASTM F1000, figure of valve accessories [S3], and ISA-5.1 for the actuator and instrument marks [S5]; cited, never copied. The valve body follows `catalog-piping-valves.md`. A motorized valve stays in place without power; a spring-return valve goes to its fail position: set `failPosition` on each valve. [S30]

## 1. Motor and hydraulic valves (actuated valves catalog)

### Motor-operated valve (vanne motorisée)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a circle or a box; a letter marks the electric drive. [S3] [S5] [S50]
- Real working: a motor drives the stem through a self-locking worm gear, so the valve does not drift; limit switches give the end positions; torque switches protect a blocked valve; a transmitter can return the position; without power it stays in place; remote operation with a position indicator. [S30] [S15]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, `stopped`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: sets `onOffValve` and `controlMode`; `actualPositionPercent` (number, %, M, with a transmitter); `hasTorqueFault`, `hasPowerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: open, close or stop order; the position moves by `100 / travelTimeS` % per second while the order lasts; limit switches at 100 % and 0 %; without power, the position freezes.

### Hydraulically operated valve (vanne à commande hydraulique)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a piston actuator; a letter marks the hydraulic drive. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: fluid pressure pushes a piston that moves the valve; a fail-safe position can be set. Public example: the ballast vent valves of a 1946 submarine are hydraulically operated, with a hand back-up. [S30] [S16]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: sets `onOffValve` and `controlMode`; `isHydraulicPressureAvailable` (boolean, M); `isHandOperated` (boolean, M).
- Model: a ramp as the motor-operated valve; no motion without hydraulic pressure, except by hand; on pressure loss, it goes to `failPosition`.

## 2. Solenoid and control valves (actuated valves catalog)

### Solenoid valve (électrovanne)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a box with the coil letter. [S3] [S5]
- Real working: a coil pulls a plunger that opens or closes the passage; a spring returns it. Direct acting: simple, fast, power hungry. Pilot operated: the coil opens a small port and line pressure moves the diaphragm, which needs enough pressure difference. [S29]
- States: `deenergized`, `energized`, plus `hasCoilFault`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, C); `isOpen` (boolean, X or M); `normalPosition` (enum `normallyClosed`, `normallyOpen`; P); `hasCoilFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: on or off, no travel time; back to `normalPosition` when power fails. [S29]

### Control valve and positioner (vanne de régulation et positionneur)

- Symbol: the valve symbol topped by a diaphragm actuator (half disk); the positioner is a small box next to the actuator. [S5] [S3]
- Real working: its opening follows the signal of a controller, acting on flow, so on pressure, temperature or level; the positioner compares the stem position with the command and corrects it despite friction and wear; a spring sends it open or closed when air fails; signals 4 to 20 mA or 3 to 15 psi. [S28]
- States: `closed`, `modulating`, `open`, `failSafe`, plus `hasPositionerFault`.
- Properties: sets `modulatingValve` and `controlMode`; `hasPositionerFault`, `hasAirSupplyFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: the position follows the command with a rise time [S34]; `Q = Kv × f(position) × √(Δp / relative density)` [S33]; on air loss, it goes to `failPosition`.

## 3. Piping parts (actuated valves catalog)

### Three-way valve (vanne trois voies)

- Symbol: the two-way valve with one more triangle: three triangles pointing to a common center. [S3] [S50]
- Real working: it splits or mixes two flows; the thermostatic valve of a lube oil circuit sets the temperature through the cooler [S22]; the three-way valve of an oily water separator sends off-spec water back to the tank [S18].
- States: `positionA`, `positionB`, `mixing`.
- Properties: `commandedPositionPercent`, `actualPositionPercent` (number, % toward port A, C and M); or `commandedPath`, `actualPath` (enum `a`, `b`) for an on-off version.
- Model: two coupled valves, opening `x` toward A and `1 − x` toward B; a thermostatic `x` follows the gap between measured and set temperature.

### Simplex and duplex strainer (filtre simple et filtre double)

- Symbol: a filter body with a dashed basket; two bodies and a change-over valve for the duplex. [S3]
- Real working: simplex, one element in a sump, cleaned by turning it against scrapers; duplex, two elements, one cleaned while the other stays in service. [S15]
- States: `clean`, `clogging`; duplex: element A or B in service.
- Properties: `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `activeElement` (enum `a`, `b`; M, duplex).
- Model: `Δp = K(clogging) × Q²` on the element in service.

### Valve manifold (collecteur de vannes)

- Symbol: a row of valves on a common body. [S3]
- Real working: it takes suction from several sources and discharges to several destinations, for example to transfer fuel between tanks; suction valves are often stop-check valves, so that pumps do not lose their prime. [S15]
- States: those of each valve.
- Properties: one valve object per way (set `onOffValve`); `selectedSource`, `selectedDestination` (string, X).
- Model: one common node at one pressure; each way is an on-off valve.

## Sources

- [S18] Wikipedia, "Oily water separator (marine)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oily_water_separator_(marine)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S50] Tameson, "Valve Symbols in Process and Instrumentation Diagrams", <https://tameson.com/pages/valve-symbols-pid>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Compressed air symbols catalog

Contents: 1. Pressure levels · 2. Production and storage · 3. Treatment · 4. Pressure and control · Sources

Symbol standards: ISO 1219-1 for pneumatic components [S1]; ISO 14617 for compressors [S2]; ASTM F1000 for ship piping, air flasks included [S3]. Cited, never copied. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. A pneumatic cylinder uses the hydraulic cylinder card; the safety valve of an air receiver is in `catalog-piping-valves.md`; submarine air is in `catalog-ship-systems.md`.

## 1. Pressure levels (ship compressed air)

| Network | Nominal pressure | Use |
| --- | --- | --- |
| High pressure (HP) | 3,000 or 5,000 psi, about 207 or 345 bar | Above 1,000 psi; starting diesel engines and gas turbines |
| Medium pressure (MP) | 151 to 1,000 psi, about 10 to 69 bar | From an MP compressor or by reducing HP air |
| Low pressure (LP) | 150 psi and less, about 10 bar | The widest network on board |

Values of one navy, converted by the writer (1 psi ≈ 0.069 bar): a use, not a rule. Each project has its own. [S13]

## 2. Production and storage (compressed air catalog)

### Air compressor (compresseur d'air)

- Symbol: a circle with a triangle pointing outward, as a pump; an open triangle for air. [S1] [S2] [S49]
- Real working: it gives the air volume and pressure asked; a receiver in line smooths its pulses and lets it run unloaded when demand is low; coolers between stages and at the outlet remove some water. [S13] [S11]
- States: `stopped`, `loaded`, `unloaded`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: set `drive`; `isLoaded` (boolean, M); `dischargePressureBar` (number, bar, M); `dischargeTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `cutInPressureBar`, `cutOutPressureBar` (number, bar, P).
- Model: constant flow when loaded, zero when unloaded; loads under `cutInPressureBar`, unloads above `cutOutPressureBar`.

### Air receiver and HP air flask (réservoir d'air et bouteille d'air haute pression)

- Symbol: ASTM F1000, figure of miscellaneous components, which covers flasks: a closed elongated shape. [S3]
- Real working: the receiver stores air for demand peaks; HP flasks add a high-pressure, high-flow reserve; receivers and flasks have low-point drains for water. [S13] [S11]
- States: `empty`, `low`, `normal`, `full`.
- Properties: `pressureBar` (number, bar, M); `volumeL` (number, L, P); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasLowPressureAlarm` (boolean, D); `isDrainOpen` (boolean, M).
- Model: ideal gas, `dp/dt = (mass inflow − mass outflow) × R × T / V`.

## 3. Treatment (compressed air catalog)

### Air dryer (sécheur d'air)

- Symbol: the diamond of conditioning devices; the navy list has a chemical dryer symbol. [S1] [S49] [S7]
- Real working: a refrigerated dryer passes air over a cold coil where water and oil condense; a desiccant dryer has two towers, one drying while the other regenerates with a reverse flow of hot dry air. Moisture corrodes, thins lubricants and freezes in cold weather. [S11] [S13]
- States: `stopped`, `drying`, `regenerating`, plus `hasDryerFault`.
- Properties: `isRunning` (boolean, M); `activeTower` (enum `a`, `b`; M, desiccant dryer); `outletDewPointDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasDryerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: first-order delay of the outlet dew point toward a target; towers switch on a timer.

### Moisture separator and air filter (séparateur d'eau et filtre à air)

- Symbol: the diamond, with a mesh or a line for the element and a drain at the bottom. [S1] [S49]
- Real working: the separator removes liquid water; the filter stops solids (screens, porous elements, swirl chambers); low-point drains open at intervals. [S11] [S13]
- States: `clean`, `clogging`; drain `open` or `closed`.
- Properties: `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `isDrainOpen` (boolean, M or C).
- Model: `Δp = K(clogging) × Q²`.

## 4. Pressure and control (compressed air catalog)

### Air pressure reducing station (poste de détente d'air)

- Symbol: ISO 1219-1 for the reducing valve (one box, spring, arrow in line, pilot from the outlet) [S1]; ASTM F1000 for its place in a piping diagram [S3].
- Real working: reducing stations lower HP air to a usable level; MP air can come from an HP flask bank through them; the valve holds a steady outlet pressure. [S13] [S9]
- States: `regulating`, `fullyOpen`, `closed`.
- Properties: `outletPressureSetpointBar` (number, bar, C); `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`.

### Pneumatic solenoid valve (électrovanne pneumatique)

- Symbol: boxes side by side with flow arrows, the coil at one end and the spring at the other; the navy list has an electric coil symbol. [S48] [S7]
- Real working: current in a coil pulls a plunger that opens or closes the passage; a spring returns it when the current stops; normally closed or normally open; direct or pilot-operated; a three-way valve links a port to supply or exhaust. [S29]
- States: `deenergized`, `energized`, plus `hasCoilFault`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, C); `isOpen` (boolean, X or M); `normalPosition` (enum `normallyClosed`, `normallyOpen`; P); `hasCoilFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: on or off; response 5 to 150 ms by type, negligible on a synoptic; back to `normalPosition` when power fails. [S29]

## Sources

- [S1] ISO, "ISO 1219-1:2012 Fluid power systems and components, Graphical symbols and circuit diagrams, Part 1", catalog page, <https://www.boutique.afnor.org/en-gb/standard/iso-121912012/fluid-power-systems-and-components-graphic-symbols-and-circuit-diagrams-par/xs104720/120728>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S2] Wikipedia, "ISO 14617", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_14617>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S7] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, appendix "Graphic Symbols for Fluid Power Diagrams", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-graphic-symbols-for-fluid-power-diagrams-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S9] Same, chapter 6 "Valves", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-valves-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S11] Same, chapter 9, <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-reservoirs-strainers-filters-accumulators-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S13] Same, chapter 11 "Pneumatics", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/pneumatics-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S29] Wikipedia, "Solenoid valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solenoid_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S49] Carr Lane Manufacturing, "Hydraulic Symbols and How to Read Them", <https://www.carrlane.com/engineering-resources/technical-information/power-workholding/design-information/hydraulic-symbols-and-diagrams>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Electrical storage, loads and power functions catalog

Contents: 1. Storage · 2. Loads and shore · 3. Power functions · Sources

Symbols come from IEC 60617, a paid database: "usual shape" is an analysis to check on the standard. Generation, switchboards and switching devices are in `catalog-electrical-power.md`. Every card also takes the common inputs of `symbol-property-naming.md`.

## 1. Storage (electrical catalog)

### Storage battery (batterie d'accumulateurs)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, alternate long and short lines; fire plan symbol IMO 2.35. [S1]
- Real working: the state of charge is the usable capacity left, 0 to 100 %; it is not measured directly but estimated from the voltage or by integrating the current, which drifts and is reset when the charger sees full charge. [S22]
- States: `charging`, `discharging`, `floating`, `depleted`.
- Properties: `voltageV` (number, V, M); `currentA` (number, A, M, positive when discharging); `stateOfChargePercent` (number, %, M); `capacityAh` (number, Ah, P); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `isCharging` (boolean, M); `timeRemainingS` (number, s, M); `hasLowVoltageAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: `SoC = SoC0 − (1 / capacity) × ∫ I dt`; voltage = function of the state of charge minus the inner drop `R × I`. Public names: Signal K `electrical.batteries.*`, IEC 61850 ZBAT. [S2] [S6]

### Battery charger (chargeur de batterie)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, the rectifier symbol linked to the battery.
- Real working: a rectifier that turns AC into DC to charge the battery [S62]; a public data model gives it a charging mode, a voltage setpoint and a current setpoint [S2].
- States: `off`, `bulk`, `absorption`, `float`, plus `hasChargerFault`.
- Properties: `isOn` (boolean, M); `chargingMode` (enum, M); `outputVoltageV`, `voltageSetpointV` (number, V, M and C); `outputCurrentA`, `currentLimitA` (number, A, M and C); `hasSupplyFault`, `hasChargerFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: constant current up to the voltage setpoint, then constant voltage.

## 2. Loads and shore (electrical catalog)

### Motor and starter (moteur et démarreur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "M"; the starter is a rectangle or the contactor with its thermal relay.
- Real working: an induction motor turns slightly slower than its field, a slip of about 0.5 to 5 %; 4 poles at 50 Hz give a 1,500 rpm field; direct-on-line start draws 6 to 7 times rated current, reduced by star-delta, autotransformer, soft starter or drive [S19]; the starter joins a contactor and protections (overload, undervoltage) [S14].
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, plus `isOverloadTripped`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning`, `isOverloadTripped` (boolean, M); `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `currentA` (number, A, M); `ratedCurrentA` (number, A, P); `startingMethod` (enum `directOnLine`, `starDelta`, `autotransformer`, `softStarter`, `variableFrequencyDrive`; P); `startCount` (integer, M); `runTimeH` (number, h, M).
- Model: current 6 to 7 × rated during the start time, then proportional to load; speed first-order to `(1 − slip) × field speed`; trips on a lasting overload. Public name: IEC 61850 ZMOT. [S6]

### Shore connection (prise de quai)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a socket and plug with their breaker.
- Real working: shore power feeds the ship at berth with engines stopped, through a suitable cable; large installations convert the voltage and frequency of the grid; IEC/ISO/IEEE 80005-1 covers ships over 1 MVA. [S20]
- States: `disconnected`, `connected`, `supplying`.
- Properties: `isCableConnected`, `isShoreSupplyAvailable`, `isShoreBreakerClosed`, `isPhaseSequenceCorrect` (boolean, M); `shoreVoltageV` (number, V, M); `shoreFrequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `shorePowerKw` (number, kW, M).
- Model: an external source of unlimited power; interlocked with the ship generators (no lasting parallel run, to confirm on the simulated ship).
- Pitfall: on a fire plan, "international shore connection" (IMO 2.14) is the fire main coupling, "raccord international de jonction avec la terre", not the electrical shore connection. [S1] [S68]

## 3. Power functions (electrical catalog)

### Load shedding (délestage)

- Symbol: a function, with no symbol of its own; on a synoptic, a mark on the non-essential feeders.
- Real working: preferential trips disconnect non-essential consumers on overload, to avoid the main breaker trip and a blackout; loads go in steps at 5, 10 and 15 seconds (air conditioning, ventilation, galley); the power management system sheds load in two steps when consumption exceeds production. [S12] [S11]
- States: `inactive`, `stage1`, `stage2`, `stage3`.
- Properties: `isLoadSheddingActive` (boolean, M); `sheddingStage` (integer, M); `overloadThresholdPercent` (number, %, P); `stageDelayS` (number, s, P); `sheddingGroup` (integer, P); `isNonEssential` (boolean, P); `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C).
- Model: when the load of a set stays above the threshold for the delay of a step, open the feeders of that shedding group.

### Synchronizing (couplage)

- Symbol: a function; usual shape on a synoptic, a synchronism indicator near the generator breaker.
- Real working: before closing a set on a live busbar, voltage, frequency, phase sequence and phase angle must match; a synchroscope shows whether the set runs fast or slow; a synchro-check relay blocks closing outside the window; an automatic synchronizer does it alone [S16]; closing out of phase gives a high current and can damage the set [S21].
- States: `idle`, `synchronizing`, `synchronized`, plus `hasSyncFailure`.
- Properties: `isSyncCommanded` (boolean, C); `isAutoSyncActive`, `isSynchronized` (boolean, M); `voltageDifferenceV` (number, V, M); `frequencyDifferenceHz` (number, Hz, M); `phaseAngleDeg` (number, °, M); `hasSyncFailure` (boolean, D).
- Model: the phase angle integrates the frequency gap, `dφ/dt = 360 × Δf`; closing allowed when the voltage, frequency and angle gaps are inside their windows.

## Sources

- [S1] International Maritime Organization, "Resolution A.952(23), Graphical symbols for shipboard fire control plans", 2004, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/OurWork/Safety/Documents/A.23_Res.952.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

- [S11] Wikipedia, "Power management system", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Power_management_system>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S12] Marine Insight, "Preferential Trips on Ship: Construction and Working", <https://www.marineinsight.com/marine-safety/preferential-trips-on-ship-construction-and-working/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

- [S21] Wikipedia, "Diesel generator", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diesel_generator>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

id: synoptics-catalog-electrical-power

# Electrical generation and distribution catalog

Contents: 1. Symbols · 2. Generation · 3. Switchboards · 4. Switching devices · 5. Transformer and converter · Sources

## 1. Symbols of the electrical catalog

- Electrical symbols come from IEC 60617, a paid database: no symbol was read for this base. "Usual shape" is an analysis: check it on the standard before drawing.
- In IEC practice a circuit breaker is a switching contact with one or more qualifier signs; the exact shape depends on the diagram type and the legend of the project. The old IEEE/ANSI 315-1975 is inactive but still in old drawings. [S69]
- On a fire control plan, the emergency generator, emergency battery and emergency switchboard have IMO symbols 2.34 to 2.36. [S1]
- Every card also takes the common inputs of `symbol-property-naming.md` (`health`, `controlLocation`, alarm priority and state). Switching devices use `switchPosition` with four values, as in IEC 61850. [S10]

## 2. Generation (electrical catalog)

### Generator set (groupe électrogène)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "G" linked to its prime mover; fire plan symbol IMO 2.34. [S1]
- Real working: a diesel engine drives an alternator; a governor holds speed, so frequency; with 4 to 5 % droop, power follows the gap between speed and setpoint, and sets with the same droop share load by their rating; the power management system starts a second set when the first reaches 80 % of its capacity. [S21] [S17] [S11]
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, `connected`, `stopping`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning` (boolean, M); `speedRpm`, `speedSetpointRpm` (number, rpm, M and C); `activePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `droopPercent` (number, %, P); `runTimeH` (number, h, M); `hasStartFault`, `hasOverspeedFault` (boolean, D); `startPriority` (integer, C).
- Model: start is a delay; speed is a first-order move to the setpoint; `P = Pn × (f0 − f) / (droop × fn)`; motion `J dω/dt = Tm − Te`. [S18] Public names: Signal K `propulsion.*`, IEC 61850 ZGEN. [S2] [S6]

### Alternator (alternateur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a circle marked "G" with the alternating current sign.
- Real working: a rotating field induces an alternating voltage in the stator; frequency depends on speed and poles (2 poles at 3,000 rpm give 50 Hz); the automatic voltage regulator sets the excitation to hold voltage and to share reactive power. [S63]
- States: `deExcited`, `excited`, `regulating`, plus `hasAvrFault`.
- Properties: `voltageV`, `voltageSetpointV` (number, V, M and C); `currentA`, `excitationCurrentA` (number, A, M); `frequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `activePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `reactivePowerKvar` (number, kvar, M); `powerFactorRatio` (number, 0 to 1, M); `windingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `hasAvrFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: voltage is a first-order move to the setpoint, with a dip at a load step; frequency = poles × rpm / 120. Public names: Signal K `electrical.ac.*`, IEC 61850 MMXU. [S2] [S6]

## 3. Switchboards (electrical catalog)

### Main switchboard (tableau principal)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; on a single-line diagram, a thick busbar line with the feeders on it.
- Real working: it takes the main generator sets and distributes power; rules ask for two main sets at least and a switchboard placed so that a casualty hits only its own space; the power management system starts and stops sets, synchronizes, shares load, sheds load, restores after a blackout and blocks large consumers. [S25] [S11]
- States: `dead`, `energized`, `overloaded`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, M); `busVoltageV` (number, V, M); `busFrequencyHz` (number, Hz, M); `totalLoadKw`, `availablePowerKw` (number, kW, M); `isBusTieClosed` (boolean, M); `hasEarthFault`, `hasBlackout` (boolean, D).
- Model: power produced = power consumed; no closed source on the busbar, zero voltage (blackout).

### Emergency switchboard and emergency source (tableau de secours et source de secours)

- Symbol: fire plan symbols IMO 2.34 (generator), 2.35 (battery), 2.36 (switchboard) [S1]; IEC 60617 on the electrical diagram.
- Real working: the emergency source is independent, above the bulkhead deck, outside machinery spaces: a battery or a set with its own fuel, starting automatically on new ships; a transitional battery holds half an hour for emergency lighting, watertight doors and alarms [S25]. One secondary course gives 45 seconds at most to take the required services (single source). [S66]
- States: `fedFromMain`, `blackout`, `emergencyStarting`, `fedFromEmergency`.
- Properties: `supplyState` (enum, M); `isFedFromMain`, `isFedFromEmergency`, `isInterconnectorClosed`, `isTransitionalSourceActive` (boolean, M); `emergencyStartDelayS` (number, s, P); `busVoltageV` (number, V, M); `hasEmergencyStartFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: loss of main supply, start delay, emergency breaker closes, emergency services fed. French official terms: [S68].

## 4. Switching devices (electrical catalog)

### Circuit breaker (disjoncteur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617, a switching contact with a qualifier sign [S69]; usual shape, an oblique open contact with a cross at its end.
- Real working: it cuts the current on overcurrent and can be reset, unlike a fuse; a bimetal reacts to long overloads, a solenoid to high currents; a spring or air parts the contacts and the arc is quenched in air, oil, SF6 or vacuum; after a fault trip it must be reset before closing. [S13]
- States: `switchPosition` `intermediate`, `open`, `closed`, `bad` (bad only when the auxiliary contacts disagree), plus `isTripped`. [S10]
- Properties: `switchPosition` (enum, M); `isClosed`, `isTripped`, `isCloseBlocked` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded`, `isOpenCommanded`, `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C); `tripCause` (enum `overload`, `shortCircuit`, `undervoltage`, `external`; M); `currentA` (number, A, M); `ratedCurrentA` (number, A, P); `isNormallyOpen` (boolean, P); `operationCount` (integer, M).
- Model: ideal switch with an operating delay; trips when the current passes a threshold for a time; stays tripped until reset. Public names: IEC 61850 XCBR and CSWI, CIM `Switch.normalOpen`. [S6] [S10] [S67]

### Contactor (contacteur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a contact with a half circle and a rectangular coil.
- Real working: a switch driven by a coil, for heavy currents (motors, lighting, heating), not made to cut a short circuit; the coil pulls a core that closes the contacts, a spring opens them; with an overload relay it makes a motor starter. [S14]
- States: `open`, `closed`, plus `hasWeldedContactFault`.
- Properties: `isClosed`, `isCoilEnergized`, `isAuxiliaryContactClosed` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded` (boolean, C); `hasWeldedContactFault` (boolean, D); `operationCount` (integer, M).
- Model: follows the command with a short delay; opens when the control voltage disappears.

### Disconnector (sectionneur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a contact with a short perpendicular line at its end.
- Real working: it isolates a circuit visibly for maintenance; it cannot quench an arc and opens only after another device has cut the current; often paired with an earthing switch, with interlocks on the order of operations. [S15]
- States: `switchPosition` `open`, `closed`, `intermediate`, `bad`, plus `isEarthed`.
- Properties: `switchPosition` (enum, M); `isClosed`, `isEarthed`, `isLocked`, `isOperationAllowed` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded`, `isOpenCommanded` (boolean, C); `hasInterlockViolation` (boolean, D).
- Model: a slow ideal switch; the operation is refused, or raises a fault, while the linked breaker is closed. Public names: IEC 61850 XSWI and CILO. [S6]

## 5. Transformer and converter (electrical catalog)

### Power transformer (transformateur de puissance)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, two overlapping circles or two windings face to face.
- Real working: a varying current in one winding makes a flux in the core that induces a voltage in the other; the voltage ratio equals the turns ratio; copper and iron losses, a high inrush current at energizing, galvanic isolation. [S61]
- States: `deEnergized`, `energized`, `overloaded`.
- Properties: `isEnergized` (boolean, M); `primaryVoltageV`, `secondaryVoltageV` (number, V, M); `turnsRatio` (number, P); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `windingTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `tapPosition` (integer, M); `hasOvertemperatureFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `V2 = V1 × N2 / N1`; constant efficiency; inrush ignored or a short peak. Public names: IEC 61850 YPTR, YLTC. [S6]

### Power converter (convertisseur)

- Symbol: IEC 60617; usual shape, a square crossed by a diagonal, with the input sign on one side and the output sign on the other.
- Real working: rectifier (AC to DC), inverter (DC to AC), chopper (DC to DC), AC to AC converter changing voltage or frequency; a drive sets the speed of an induction motor; switching injects harmonics to filter. [S62]
- States: `off`, `starting`, `running`, `currentLimited`, plus `hasConverterFault`.
- Properties: `converterType` (enum `rectifier`, `inverter`, `dcDc`, `frequencyConverter`; P); `isStartCommanded`, `isStopCommanded` (boolean, C); `isRunning` (boolean, M); `inputVoltageV`, `outputVoltageV` (number, V, M); `outputFrequencyHz`, `outputFrequencySetpointHz` (number, Hz, M and C); `outputCurrentA` (number, A, M); `loadPercent` (number, %, M); `hasConverterFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: output = setpoint through a ramp; constant efficiency; current limit. Public names: IEC 61850 ZCON, Signal K `electrical.inverters.*`. [S6] [S2]

# Fire control plan symbols and fire detection catalog

Contents: 1. Fire control plan symbols · 2. Detectors · 3. Call point and alarm · Sources

The encyclopedia pages cited describe building equipment; ship rules come from IMO and flag texts [S1] [S37] [S38]. Extinguishing systems, fire doors and fire dampers are in `catalog-fire-extinguishing.md` and `catalog-hvac.md`.

## 1. Fire control plan symbols (IMO A.952(23))

The reference for a ship fire control plan is IMO resolution A.952(23), adopted on 2003-12-05, not ISO 7010. [S1]

| Group | Numbers | Content |
| --- | --- | --- |
| Structural fire protection | 1.1 to 1.18 | Class A and B divisions, main vertical zone, fire doors (hinged or sliding, self-closing or not), remote controls, fire damper, ventilation closures |
| Fire-protection appliances | 2.1 to 2.44 | Fire pumps and controls, remote stops of fuel and oil pumps, remote release station, international shore connection, hydrants, section valves, fixed systems, extinguishers, emergency sources and switchboard, detection panel, general alarm, call point, spaces watched by smoke, heat, flame or gas detectors |
| Means of escape | 3.1 to 3.3 | Primary and secondary escape routes, emergency escape breathing device |

- Ventilation codes (1.12, 1.15 to 1.18): circle colour and letter, blue "A" accommodation and service spaces, green "M" machinery spaces, yellow "C" cargo spaces. [S1]
- Extinguishing agent codes: grey CO2 or nitrogen, brown "H" other gases, white "P" powder, green "W" water, yellow "F" foam, orange (2.32) sprinklers or high-pressure water. [S1]
- Every plan carries a legend of the symbols it uses; ships built before 2004-01-01 may keep the symbols of resolution A.654(16). [S1]
- ISO 17631:2002, cited by the resolution, was withdrawn on 2022-12-07 and replaced by ISO 17631:2022, which adds damage control. [S28]
- ISO 7010 signs (red square, F001 to F019) are the signs fixed on board, another family. [S26]
- EN 54 parts: 2 control panel, 3 sounders, 5 heat, 7 smoke, 10 flame detectors, 11 call points, 23 visual alarm devices. [S27]

## 2. Detectors (fire detection catalog)

Each detector also has `loopId`, `deviceAddress` and `zoneId` (string, P) when it is addressable.

### Smoke detector (détecteur de fumée)

- Symbol: IMO 2.41, space watched by smoke detectors [S1]; component EN 54-7 [S27].
- Real working: optical, a light source and a photodiode in a chamber, smoke scatters light to the sensor; ionization, smoke lowers the current of an ionized chamber against a reference; an addressable detector has its own number, so the panel knows which one is in alarm and can report contamination; dust and cooking fumes cause false alarms [S29]. On board, smoke detectors are in stairways, corridors and escape routes of accommodation spaces [S37].
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm`, `isContaminated` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `smokeLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `alarmThresholdPercent` (number, %, P).
- Model: smoke level of the space, first-order delay, threshold, alarm latched until the panel reset. Public name: IFC `SMOKESENSOR`. [S59]

### Heat detector (détecteur de chaleur)

- Symbol: IMO 2.42 [S1]; component EN 54-5 [S27].
- Real working: fixed temperature, an alloy melts at its rating, often 58 °C in buildings; rate-of-rise, it reacts to a rise of about 6.7 to 8.3 °C per minute whatever the start; every heat detector lags the room air. [S30]
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `fixedThresholdDegC` (number, °C, P); `rateOfRiseThresholdDegCPerMin` (number, °C/min, P); `responseTimeS` (number, s, P).
- Model: element temperature, first-order delay of the room temperature; alarm above the threshold or when the rise passes the rate threshold. Public name: IFC `HEATSENSOR`. [S59]

### Flame detector (détecteur de flamme)

- Symbol: IMO 2.43 [S1]; component EN 54-10 [S27].
- Real working: it senses flame radiation, ultraviolet, infrared or both against false alarms; ultraviolet reacts in milliseconds but a 2 to 3 second delay is often added; infrared answers in 3 to 5 seconds; arc welding, sun and hot surfaces fool it; water or oil on the lens blinds it. [S31]
- States: `normal`, `alarm`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isAlarm`, `isLensObscured` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled` (boolean, C); `detectorType` (enum `uv`, `ir`, `uvIr`, `multiIr`; P); `responseDelayS` (number, s, P).
- Model: flame in the field of view (boolean), response delay, alarm latched. Public name: IFC `FIRESENSOR`. [S59]

## 3. Call point and alarm (fire detection catalog)

### Manual call point (déclencheur manuel)

- Symbol: IMO 2.40 [S1]; sign ISO 7010 F005 [S26]; component EN 54-11 [S27].
- Real working: a person presses a button or breaks a glass to raise the alarm; a light shows the point used; it is reset by hand with a key [S32]. On board: in accommodation, service spaces and control stations, one at each exit, no point of a corridor more than 20 m from one [S37].
- States: `normal`, `activated`, `disabled`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isActivated` (boolean, M); `hasFault` (boolean, D); `isDisabled`, `isLocalResetCommanded` (boolean, C).
- Model: a latched boolean until the local reset, then the panel reset. Public names: IFC `BREAKGLASSBUTTON`, `MANUALPULLBOX`. [S59]

### Sounder and beacon (sirène et flash)

- Symbol: sign ISO 7010 F018 [S26]; components EN 54-3 and EN 54-23 [S27]; no IMO symbol of its own, next to 2.39 general alarm [S1].
- Real working: audible (bells, horns, electronic sirens), visible (strobes) or voice devices; the panel sets the tone pattern [S39]. On board, a sounder outside the detection system needs two supplies, one of them emergency [S38].
- States: `off`, `sounding`, `silenced`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isSoundCommanded` (boolean, C); `isSounding`, `isFlashing`, `isSilenced` (boolean, M); `tonePattern` (enum, P); `hasFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: follows the panel output; silencing stops the sound, not the alarm.

### Fire detection control panel (centrale de détection incendie)

- Symbol: IMO 2.38 [S1]; component EN 54-2 [S27].
- Real working: it takes the detector signals, checks that they work and drives the linked equipment by a set sequence; conventional, it knows the zone; addressable, each device on its loops [S33]. On board it shows doors, detectors, alarms and fans; an open detector circuit gives an alarm; a detector can stop fans and close fire doors and dampers [S37].
- States: `normal`, `preAlarm`, `alarm`, `alarmSilenced`, `fault`. [S33]
- Properties: `panelState` (enum, M); `activeAlarmCount`, `activeFaultCount`, `disabledDeviceCount` (integer, M); `firstAlarmZoneId` (string, M); `isAcknowledgeCommanded`, `isSilenceCommanded`, `isResetCommanded` (boolean, C); `isOnBackupPower`, `isVentilationStopOutputActive`, `isDoorReleaseOutputActive` (boolean, M); `hasPowerFault`, `hasLoopFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: a state machine over the devices; commands acknowledge, silence, reset; outputs sounders, door release, damper closing, ventilation stop. French official term: [S68].

# Fire extinguishing systems and fire doors catalog

Contents: 1. Gas and water systems · 2. Foam and sprinklers · 3. Fire doors and dampers · Sources

Fire plan symbols and their colour codes: `catalog-fire-detection.md`. Pumps and valves of these systems: the fluid catalogs.

## 1. Gas and water systems (fire extinguishing catalog)

### CO2 fixed gas system (extinction fixe au CO2)

- Symbol: IMO 2.13 remote release station, 2.20 fixed system, 2.21 fixed battery, 2.22 bottle in the protected space, 2.32 protected space; grey for CO2 [S1]; signs ISO 7010 F008, F012, F013, F014 [S26].
- Real working: in total flooding, gas fills a closed space to the extinguishing concentration through bottles, release valves, pipes and nozzles; CO2 can suffocate people [S35]. On board, two separate controls are used in order: one opens the valve of the pipe to the space, the other releases the bottles; a pre-discharge alarm starts by itself, for example when the cabinet opens, and sounds at least 20 seconds before the gas [S50].
- States: `standby`, `preDischargeAlarm`, `valveOpen`, `discharging`, `discharged`, plus `hasSystemFault`.
- Properties: `systemState` (enum, M); `isReleaseCabinetOpen`, `isPreDischargeAlarmActive`, `isDistributionValveOpen`, `isDischarged` (boolean, M); `preDischargeDelayS` (number, s, P); `isDistributionValveOpenCommanded`, `isReleaseCommanded` (boolean, C); `protectedSpaceId` (string, P); `hasSystemFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: cabinet open, alarm and delay, valve open, discharge, space flooded; release refused while the valve is closed. French official term: [S68].

### Water mist system (extinction par brouillard d'eau)

- Symbol: IMO 2.24 water spray section valves, 2.33 water mist lance, 2.32 protected space in orange for high-pressure water. [S1]
- Real working: droplets under 1,000 microns cool, lower the oxygen near the flame and block radiation; deluge (all nozzles open), wet pipe, dry pipe or pre-action. [S36]
- States: `standby`, `activated`, `discharging`, plus `hasLowPressureAlarm`.
- Properties: `isActivateCommanded` (boolean, C); `isActivated`, `isSectionValveOpen`, `isPumpRunning` (boolean, M); `systemPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `releaseMode` (enum `automatic`, `manual`; P); `hasLowPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: activation, pump start, pressure rise (first order), discharge in the open section.

## 2. Foam and sprinklers (fire extinguishing catalog)

### Foam system (extinction à mousse)

- Symbol: IMO 2.19 foam section valve, 2.23 high-expansion foam duct, 2.30 portable foam unit; yellow, letter "F". [S1]
- Real working: foam cools the fire and covers the fuel with a layer that cuts oxygen; expansion ratio low under 20, medium 20 to 200, high above 200 up to 1,000; high expansion fills a closed space fast. [S40]
- States: `standby`, `discharging`, `empty`, plus `hasFault`.
- Properties: `isStartCommanded` (boolean, C); `isDischarging`, `isFoamPumpRunning`, `isFoamSectionValveOpen` (boolean, M); `foamConcentrateLevelPercent` (number, %, M); `proportioningRatioPercent` (number, %, P); `expansionType` (enum `low`, `medium`, `high`; P); `hasFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: concentrate flow = water flow × proportioning ratio; the concentrate tank level falls during discharge.

### Sprinkler system (sprinkleurs)

- Symbol: IMO 2.17 sprinkler section valve; 2.32 protected space, orange. [S1]
- Real working: in a wet pipe system, pipes stay full; each head opens alone when its glass bulb or fusible element reaches its rating (red bulb 68 °C); alarm switches can watch the section valves [S36]; the panel reports a closed sprinkler valve as a supervisory state [S33].
- States: `standby`, `flowing`, `isolated`, plus `hasLowPressureAlarm`.
- Properties: `isSectionValveOpen`, `isFlowDetected`, `isPumpRunning` (boolean, M); `systemPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `headRatingDegC` (number, °C, P); `openHeadCount` (integer, M); `hasLowPressureAlarm` (boolean, D).
- Model: a head opens when the local temperature reaches its rating, pressure drops, the pump starts, flow alarm.

## 3. Fire doors and dampers (fire extinguishing catalog)

### Fire door (porte coupe-feu)

- Symbol: IMO 1.4 to 1.11, class A or B door, hinged or sliding, self-closing or not; 1.14 remote control of watertight or fire doors [S1]; sign ISO 7010 F007 [S26].
- Real working: the door keeps the fire division; on a passenger ship the fire doors are closed remotely from the safety center, whose panel shows their state; a detector can close them. [S37]
- States: `open`, `closing`, `closed`, plus `hasClosingFault`.
- Properties: `isClosed`, `isHeldOpen` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded` (boolean, C); `doorClass` (enum `A`, `B`; P); `doorType` (enum `hinged`, `sliding`; P); `isSelfClosing` (boolean, P); `closingTimeS` (number, s, P); `hasClosingFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: release (command or alarm), closing in a set time, closed limit switch; fault when it does not arrive.

Fire dampers (IMO 1.15, remote control 1.17, circle colour and letter by the space served [S1]) use the card of `catalog-hvac.md`, plus `spaceCategory` (enum `accommodation`, `machinery`, `cargo`; P) and `fusibleElementRatingDegC` (number, °C, P). A fire damper stops fire crossing a fire-resisting wall or deck through a duct; a thermal element melts and springs close the blades; the fire alarm system can also close it. [S34]

## Sources

- [S1] International Maritime Organization, "Resolution A.952(23), Graphical symbols for shipboard fire control plans", 2004, <https://wwwcdn.imo.org/localresources/en/OurWork/Safety/Documents/A.23_Res.952.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S26] Wikipedia, "ISO 7010", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_7010>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S33] Wikipedia, "Fire alarm control panel", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_alarm_control_panel>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S34] Wikipedia, "Fire damper", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_damper>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S35] Wikipedia, "Gaseous fire suppression", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gaseous_fire_suppression>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S36] Wikipedia, "Fire sprinkler system", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_sprinkler_system>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S37] Maritime and Coastguard Agency, "Regulation 7, Detection and Alarm" (SOLAS II-2 with guidance), <https://assets.publishing.service.gov.uk/media/5a802a5ced915d74e622cda8/Regulation_7_-_Detection_and_Alarm.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S40] Wikipedia, "Firefighting foam", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firefighting_foam>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

- [S68] French Ministry for the Sea, "Division 221", edition of 2023-07-26, <https://mer.gouv.fr/sites/default/files/2023-08/d221_%2826-07-23%29.pdf>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Ventilation and air conditioning catalog

Contents: 1. Fan and dampers · 2. Chilled water · 3. Air units · Sources

Symbol standards: ASTM F856 for shipboard heating, ventilation and air conditioning [S6]; ISO 14617-8 for dampers and -9 for fans [S2]. The ship piping standard ASTM F1000 leaves ventilation out [S3]. Air flow in m³/h, air pressure in pascals. Roles and shared sets: `docs/synoptics/fluid-common.md`. Submarine ventilation has no public source in this base.

## 1. Fan and dampers (ventilation catalog)

### Fan (ventilateur)

- Symbol: a circle with a stylized propeller, or a circle with a tangent outlet for a centrifugal fan. [S6] [S2]
- Real working: it moves air in the ducts; in an air handling unit it is the supply fan, sometimes with a return fan; a variable speed drive sets the air flow. [S25]
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, `stopping`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: sets `drive` and `controlMode`; `airFlowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `differentialPressurePa` (number, Pa, M).
- Model: pressure-flow curve at rated speed, scaled by the affinity laws: flow as speed, pressure as its square, power as its cube [S40]; network `Δp = K × Q²`.

### Damper (registre)

- Symbol: an oblique line in the duct, or parallel blades; the actuator is drawn above. [S6] [S2]
- Real working: a valve or plate that stops or sets the air flow in a duct; by hand, or by an electric or pneumatic actuator from a controller; on-off or intermediate, some report their position; with a return spring it takes a set position when power fails. [S26]
- States: `closed`, `opening`, `open`, `closing`, `intermediate`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: set `onOffValve` for on-off, set `modulatingValve` without `kvM3PerH` for control.
- Model: variable resistance `Δp = K(position) × Q²`; zero flow when closed; a ramp over `travelTimeS`.

### Fire damper (clapet coupe-feu)

- Symbol: the damper symbol marked as a fire damper by a letter or a thick line on the wall it crosses. [S6]
- Real working: it sits where a duct crosses a fire wall; a fusible element holds it open; heat melts the element and a spring closes it; a fire detection signal can close it too; it is tripped by hand to test it and reset by hand through a hatch; a smoke damper blocks smoke, a combination damper does both. [S27] [S26]
- States: `open`, `closed`, `trippedThermal`, plus `hasPositionFault`.
- Properties: `isOpen`, `isClosed` (boolean, M); `isCloseCommanded` (boolean, C); `isThermallyTripped` (boolean, M); `isResetRequired` (boolean, X); `hasPositionFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: open at start; closed on a close order or when the local temperature passes a threshold; after a thermal trip, closed until reset by hand.

## 2. Chilled water (ventilation catalog)

### Chiller (groupe froid)

- Symbol: an assembly rectangle with compressor, condenser and evaporator; four connections, chilled water supply and return, condenser water supply and return. [S6]
- Real working: it removes heat from a liquid, mostly water, by a vapor compression or absorption cycle; in compression, the expansion valve splits high and low pressure sides and meters the refrigerant, the evaporator absorbs heat, the compressor draws and compresses the vapor, the condenser makes it liquid again, a receiver stores the liquid; chilled water about 7 °C supply and 12 °C return; air or water cooled condenser. [S24] [S23]
- States: `stopped`, `starting`, `running`, plus `hasChillerFault`.
- Properties: sets `drive` and `controlMode`; `chilledWaterSetpointDegC` (number, °C, C); `chilledWaterSupplyTemperatureDegC`, `chilledWaterReturnTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `capacityPercent` (number, %, M); `hasChillerFault`, `hasNoFlowFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: running, the supply temperature moves toward the setpoint with a first-order delay while the load stays under the capacity; stopped, it rises toward the return temperature; safety stop without water flow. [S24]

### Cooling and heating coil (batterie froide et batterie chaude)

- Symbol: a rectangle crossed by a diagonal in the duct, with a control valve on its water supply. [S6]
- Real working: the coil heats or cools the air with hot water, steam, chilled water or a direct expansion refrigerant; the cooling coil also dries the air; a motorized valve sets the water flow. [S25] [S24]
- States: those of its valve.
- Properties: valve, set `modulatingValve`; coil, `airInletTemperatureDegC`, `airOutletTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `waterFlowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M).
- Model: air outlet = air inlet − effectiveness × valve opening × (air inlet − water inlet); the same form for heating.

## 3. Air units (ventilation catalog)

### Air handling unit (centrale de traitement d'air)

- Symbol: a large rectangle crossed by the duct, with in order the damper, filter, coil and fan symbols. [S6]
- Real working: a casing that conditions and moves air: mixing box and dampers (return, outside and exhaust air), filters, heating and cooling coils, humidifier, supply fan, sometimes heat recovery and silencers; control by drives, damper actuators, motorized coil valves and sensors; the filter pressure loss tells when to change them. [S25]
- States: `stopped`, `running`, plus `hasFilterCloggedAlarm`.
- Properties: one fan object, damper objects and valve objects; `supplyAirTemperatureDegC`, `returnAirTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `supplyAirTemperatureSetpointDegC` (number, °C, C); `filterDifferentialPressurePa` (number, Pa, M); `hasFilterCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `outsideAirDamperPositionPercent` (number, %, M).
- Model: supply temperature = mix of return and outside air by damper position, then the coils by their valve opening, with a first-order delay.

### Fan coil unit (ventilo-convecteur)

- Symbol: a small local rectangle with a fan and a coil. [S6]
- Real working: a small local unit with a filter, a coil and a fan, fed with chilled water from the chiller. [S25] [S24]
- States: `off`, `running`.
- Properties: `isRunCommanded`, `isRunning` (boolean, C and M); `fanSpeedLevel` (enum `off`, `low`, `medium`, `high`; C); `roomTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `roomTemperatureSetpointDegC` (number, °C, C).
- Model: the room temperature moves toward the setpoint while the unit runs and chilled water is there.

## Sources

- [S2] Wikipedia, "ISO 14617", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ISO_14617>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S3] ASTM International, "F1000-21 Standard Practice for Piping System Drawing Symbols", 2021, <https://store.astm.org/f1000-21.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S6] ASTM International, "F856-97(2022) Standard Practice for Mechanical Symbols, Shipboard, Heating, Ventilation, and Air Conditioning", <https://store.astm.org/f0856-97r22.html>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S23] US Navy, "Fireman", NAVEDTRA 14104, chapter 10, page on refrigeration, <https://tpub.com/fireman/91.htm>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S24] Wikipedia, "Chiller", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Chiller>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S25] Wikipedia, "Air handler", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Air_handler>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S26] Wikipedia, "Damper (flow)", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Damper_(flow)>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S27] Wikipedia, "Fire damper", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fire_damper>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S40] Wikipedia, "Affinity laws", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Affinity_laws>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.

tool: none
version: "none"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S7, S8, S9, S10, S11, S12, S14, S32, S33, S34, S48, S49]

# Hydraulic symbols catalog

Contents: 1. Pumps and motor · 2. Cylinder and valves · 3. Pressure and flow valves · 4. Storage and conditioning · 5. Servo valve and cooler · Sources

How to read a card: the symbol standard is ISO 1219-1 for the whole family, cited, never copied [S1]. A symbol shows ports, flow paths and function, never the construction; it is drawn at rest and can be rotated [S8]. Roles: P parameter, C command or setpoint, M measured, D fault, X derived. The property sets `drive`, `tank`, `onOffValve`, `modulatingValve` and `processPoint` are in `docs/synoptics/catalog-fluid-common.md`. A shape or a law without [Sn] is an analysis of the writer: check it on the standard the team owns.

## 1. Pumps and motor (hydraulic catalog)

### Fixed displacement pump (pompe à cylindrée fixe)

- Symbol: a circle with a filled triangle pointing outward, in the flow direction. [S49]
- Real working: the pump gives a flow; pressure comes only from the resistance downstream. It moves the same volume at each turn: gears, vanes, screws or pistons. [S10]
- States: `stopped`, `running`, plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: set `drive`; `displacementCm3` (number, cm³/rev, P); `flowRateLPerMin` (number, L/min, M); `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: inputs speed and run order, output flow. `Q = displacement × n × volumetric efficiency`. The downstream network gives the pressure; the relief valve caps it.

### Variable displacement pump (pompe à cylindrée variable)

- Symbol: the pump symbol with an extra arrow across it for the adjustment. [S48]
- Real working: an inner part sets the volume per turn (swash plate angle, eccentricity). A pressure-compensated pump lowers its flow as the pressure nears its setting, down to zero. [S10]
- States: `stopped`, `running`, `standby` (pressure held, no flow), plus `hasTripFault`.
- Properties: set `drive`; `commandedDisplacementPercent`, `actualDisplacementPercent` (number, %, C and M); `pressureSetpointBar` (number, bar, C); `flowRateLPerMin` (number, L/min, M); `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: `Q = x × maximum displacement × n`, with `x` from 0 to 1; with compensation, `x` falls to 0 as `p` reaches `pressureSetpointBar`.

### Hydraulic motor (moteur hydraulique)

- Symbol: a circle with a filled triangle pointing inward; two triangles for two directions of rotation. [S49]
- Real working: the motor turns a flow into rotation; its build is that of a pump. With a fixed displacement, speed follows the inlet flow and torque stays constant. [S12]
- States: `stopped`, `rotatingForward`, `rotatingReverse`, `stalled`.
- Properties: `actualSpeedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `rotationDirection` (enum `forward`, `reverse`, `none`; M); `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `torqueNm` (number, N·m, X); `displacementCm3` (number, cm³/rev, P).
- Model: `n = Q / displacement`; `torque = Δp × displacement / (2π)`.

## 2. Cylinder and valves (hydraulic catalog)

### Hydraulic cylinder (vérin hydraulique)

- Symbol: a rectangle with a piston line; the return spring of a single-acting cylinder is shown. The same symbol serves in pneumatics. [S48] [S7]
- Real working: single-acting, the fluid pushes one way and a spring or gravity brings back; double-acting, the fluid acts on both sides, with a smaller area on the rod side, so a smaller force. [S12] [S14]
- States: `retracted`, `extending`, `extended`, `retracting`, `stopped`.
- Properties: `isExtended`, `isRetracted` (boolean, M); `actualPositionMm`, `commandedPositionMm` (number, mm, M and C); `capEndPressureBar`, `rodEndPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `strokeMm` (number, mm, P); `pistonAreaCm2`, `rodAreaCm2` (number, cm², P); `rodForceN` (number, N, X).
- Model: `F = p × A`, rod side area = piston area − rod area; `v = Q / A`; position = integral of `v`, bounded by 0 and `strokeMm`. [S14]

### Directional control valve (distributeur)

- Symbol: boxes side by side, one per position, with arrows for the paths; controls (solenoid, spring, lever) at the ends; drawn at rest; ports P, T, A, B. [S48] [S49] [S8]
- Real working: a spool slides in a body and lines up the ports. A four-port valve has two working positions and a center: it drives an actuator both ways. Manual, electric or pilot control. [S9]
- States: for a 4/3 valve, `center`, `positionA` (P to A, B to T), `positionB` (P to B, A to T), plus `hasSpoolFault`.
- Properties: `commandedSpoolPosition`, `actualSpoolPosition` (enum `center`, `a`, `b`; C and M); `isSolenoidAEnergized`, `isSolenoidBEnergized` (boolean, C); `centerType` (enum `closed`, `open`, `tandem`, `float`; P); `hasSpoolFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: each open path is an orifice, `Q = Kv × √(Δp / relative density)` [S33]; switching time ignored or a first-order delay.

### Check valve (clapet antiretour)

- Symbol: a ball on a V seat; a dashed pilot line for the pilot-operated version, "pilot to open" or "pilot to close". [S7]
- Real working: it opens in the free direction and closes in reverse, helped by a spring or gravity. [S9] A pilot pressure opens or closes the piloted version.
- States: `closed`, `open`, `pilotedOpen`.
- Properties: `isOpen` (boolean, X); `crackingPressureBar` (number, bar, P); `isPilotPressurized` (boolean, M, piloted version).
- Model: no flow if `Δp < crackingPressureBar` or if the flow is reversed; else an orifice. A public valve model has the same option. [S34]

## 3. Pressure and flow valves (hydraulic catalog)

### Pressure relief valve (limiteur de pression)

- Symbol: one box with a spring and an arrow offset from the line (closed at rest); the pilot line comes from the inlet. [S48]
- Real working: a spring holds the poppet closed; it lifts when the pressure passes the setting, sends fluid to return, and closes a little below the setting. A two-stage version chatters less. [S9]
- States: `closed`, `relieving`.
- Properties: `setPressureBar` (number, bar, P); `isRelieving` (boolean, X); `inletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: `Q = K × max(0, p − setPressureBar)`. On the synoptic, the circuit pressure never passes the setting.

### Pressure reducing valve (réducteur de pression)

- Symbol: one box, a spring and an arrow in line with the flow (open at rest); the pilot line comes from the outlet.
- Real working: it holds a lower, steady outlet pressure whatever the inlet pressure or the load; spring, pilot or dome control. [S9]
- States: `regulating`, `fullyOpen` (inlet under the setting), `closed`.
- Properties: `outletPressureSetpointBar` (number, bar, C); `inletPressureBar`, `outletPressureBar` (number, bar, M).
- Model: `outlet pressure = min(inlet pressure, setpoint)`.

### Flow control valve (limiteur de débit)

- Symbol: a narrowing of the line; an arrow across it when adjustable; "pressure and temperature compensated" is a distinct symbol. [S48] [S7]
- Real working: a tapered needle enters a small seat and sets the flow finely, to slow a motion or protect an instrument. [S9]
- States: no discrete state; continuous opening.
- Properties: `openingPercent` (number, %, P or C); `flowRateLPerMin` (number, L/min, M).
- Model: `Q = Kv(opening) × √(Δp / relative density)` [S33]; compensated version: flow equal to its setting.

## 4. Storage and conditioning (hydraulic catalog)

### Accumulator (accumulateur)

- Symbol: a closed elongated shape split by a line between gas and fluid; bladder, piston and diaphragm types differ. [S48] [S7]
- Real working: it stores fluid under gas pressure, holds pressure during slow leaks, damps shocks, gives peak flow and backs up the supply. The gas, often nitrogen, is precharged. [S11]
- States: `empty`, `charging`, `charged`, `discharging`.
- Properties: `pressureBar` (number, bar, M); `prechargePressureBar` (number, bar, P); `gasVolumeL` (number, L, P); `oilVolumeL` (number, L, X); `hasLowPrechargeFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: gas `p × V^n = constant`, `n = 1` when slow, about 1.4 when fast; fluid volume = total volume − gas volume.

### Filter and strainer (filtre et crépine)

- Symbol: a diamond, the shape of conditioning devices, with lines across it for a filter. [S49] [S7]
- Real working: a strainer stops coarse particles, often at the pump suction; clogged, it starves the pump. A filter stops fine particles; a bypass valve opens when the element clogs; an indicator follows the differential pressure. [S11]
- States: `clean`, `clogging`, `bypassing`.
- Properties: `differentialPressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasCloggedAlarm` (boolean, D); `isBypassing` (boolean, M); `cloggingPercent` (number, %, P, for fault injection).
- Model: `Δp = K(clogging) × Q²`; bypass open above a `Δp` threshold.

### Reservoir (réservoir hydraulique)

- Symbol: the standard tells an open reservoir from a closed one, vented or not [S48]; the exact shape is to check on the standard or the legend of the project.
- Real working: it keeps the fluid reserve, sheds heat, lets dirt settle and air escape; it has a vent, a level gauge and baffles; it is pressurized when the atmosphere cannot feed the pump. [S11]
- States: `low`, `normal`, `high`.
- Properties: set `tank`; `isPressurized` (boolean, P).
- Model: `dV/dt = inflow − outflow`; `level = V / area`.

## 5. Servo valve and cooler (hydraulic catalog)

### Servo valve (servovalve)

- Symbol: the directional valve symbol with two parallel lines along the boxes: continuously variable position. [S7]
- Real working: the spool moves in proportion to an electric signal and varies the flow from zero to maximum; a first stage (torque motor, flapper nozzle, jet) drives the second-stage spool; it often works in a closed loop. [S32] A proportional valve works alike (analysis).
- States: `null`, `flowingToA`, `flowingToB`, plus `hasValveFault`.
- Properties: `commandedSpoolPositionPercent`, `actualSpoolPositionPercent` (number, −100 to 100 %, C and M); `hasValveFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `Q = Kv × u × √(Δp / relative density)` with `u` from −1 to 1 [S33]; first-order delay on `u`.

### Hydraulic oil cooler (refroidisseur d'huile)

- Symbol: a diamond with triangles: inside for heat removed, outside for the coolant, liquid or gas. [S49] [S7]
- Real working: it sends to water or air the heat that the circuit losses give to the oil (analysis from the name and the symbol).
- States: no discrete state.
- Properties: `inletTemperatureDegC`, `outletTemperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `coolantFlowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M).
- Model: first-order delay toward a target temperature; no exchange when the coolant flow is zero.

## Sources

- [S1] ISO, "ISO 1219-1:2012 Fluid power systems and components, Graphical symbols and circuit diagrams, Part 1", catalog page, <https://www.boutique.afnor.org/en-gb/standard/iso-121912012/fluid-power-systems-and-components-graphic-symbols-and-circuit-diagrams-par/xs104720/120728>, read 2026-11-12, all rights reserved, cited by reference.
- [S7] US Navy, "Fluid Power", NAVEDTRA 14105A, appendix "Graphic Symbols for Fluid Power Diagrams", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-graphic-symbols-for-fluid-power-diagrams-navy>, read 2026-11-12, US government work, summarized.
- [S8] Same, chapter 12 "Basic Diagrams and Systems", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-basic-diagrams-and-systems-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S9] Same, chapter 6 "Valves", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-valves-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S10] Same, chapter 4 "Pumps", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-pumps-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S11] Same, chapter 9 "Reservoirs, Strainers, Filters, and Accumulators", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-reservoirs-strainers-filters-accumulators-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S12] Same, chapter 10 "Actuators", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulic-actuators-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S14] Same, chapter 2 "Forces in Liquids", <https://engineeringlibrary.org/reference/hydraulics-forces-in-liquids-navy>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S32] Wikipedia, "Electrohydraulic servo valve", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electrohydraulic_servo_valve>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA, summarized.
- [S33] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid User's Guide, Valve characteristics", <https://doc.modelica.org/om/Modelica.Fluid.UsersGuide.ComponentDefinition.ValveCharacteristics.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S34] Modelica Association, "Modelica.Fluid.Valves", Modelica Standard Library 4.0.0, <https://doc.modelica.org/Modelica%204.0.0/Resources/helpDymola/Modelica_Fluid_Valves.html>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S48] STAUFF, "Correctly reading circuit symbols according to DIN ISO 1219", 2025-08-19, <https://stauff.co.uk/en/blog/products/correctly-reading-circuit-symbols-according-to-din-iso-1219>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S49] Carr Lane Manufacturing, "Hydraulic Symbols and How to Read Them", <https://www.carrlane.com/engineering-resources/technical-information/power-workholding/design-information/hydraulic-symbols-and-diagrams>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

# Sensors and instrumentation catalog

Contents: 1. Tag letters · 2. Transmitter properties · 3. Transmitters · 4. Switches and loop · 5. Alarm limits and health · 6. Transmitter model · Sources

## 1. Tag letters (ISA 5.1 instrument tags)

An instrument tag has letters and a loop number. The first letter is the measured quantity, the next letters the function: `FIC045` is a flow indicating controller in loop 045. ISA 5.1 is cited, never copied. [S1]

| Letter | As first letter | As next letter |
| --- | --- | --- |
| P | Pressure | — |
| T | Temperature | Transmit |
| L | Level | Low |
| F | Flow rate | — |
| S | Speed, frequency | Switch |
| Z | Position | Actuator |
| A | Analysis | Alarm |
| H | Hand | High |
| I | Current | Indicate |
| C | — | Control |

Common tags: `PT`, `TT`, `LT`, `FT` transmitters, `PI` pressure indicator, `PSH` pressure switch high, `LAHH` level alarm high-high. Show the tag as given in `tagName`; never build it. [S1]

## 2. Transmitter properties (instrument properties in code)

Wrong:

```ts
interface PressureTransmitterWrong {
  pressure: number;
  alarm: boolean;
  status: number;
  simulated: boolean;
}
```

Right:

```ts
type Health = 'normal' | 'failure' | 'checkFunction' | 'offSpec' | 'maintenanceRequired';
type LimitState = 'normal' | 'lowLow' | 'low' | 'high' | 'highHigh';

interface PressureTransmitter {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly pressureBar: number;
  readonly rangeMinBar: number;
  readonly rangeMaxBar: number;
  readonly loopCurrentMa: number;
  readonly dampingS: number;
  readonly limitState: LimitState;
  readonly highHighLimitBar: number;
  readonly highLimitBar: number;
  readonly lowLimitBar: number;
  readonly lowLowLimitBar: number;
  readonly alarmDeadbandBar: number;
  readonly health: Health;
  readonly isSimulated: boolean;
  readonly simulatedPressureBar: number;
  readonly hasWireBreakFault: boolean;
}
```

These names follow public device models: a measured value with its unit, a simulation value and state, a damping time [S2]; four alarm limits with a deadband [S3]; a device health with five values [S4]. `alarmState` and `alarmPriority` of the naming document stay for the alarm itself.

## 3. Transmitters (instrumentation catalog)

Each transmitter also has the properties of section 2 for its quantity: range, loop current, damping, limits, health, simulation.

### Pressure transmitter (transmetteur de pression)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `PT` [S1]; the symbol of the project legend.
- Real working: a strain gauge bridge or a capacitive cell turns pressure into a signal; absolute, gauge or differential measure. [S7]
- States: `normal`, `underRange`, `overRange`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `pressureBar` (number, bar, M); `hasWireBreakFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: first-order lag of the process pressure (section 6).

### Temperature transmitter (transmetteur de température)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `TT`. [S1]
- Real working: a Pt100 probe has 100 Ω at 0 °C and gains about 0.385 Ω per °C; three-wire mounting reduces the wire error; the probe sits in a thermowell and its response time is the time to reach 63.2 % of a step; a thermocouple covers a wider range, faster and less precise. [S7]
- States: `normal`, `underRange`, `overRange`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `temperatureDegC` (number, °C, M); `responseTimeS` (number, s, P); `hasWireBreakFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: first-order lag with the probe response time.

### Level transmitter (transmetteur de niveau)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `LT`. [S1]
- Real working: a continuous level from the bottom pressure converted with the density, or radar, ultrasound, magnetostrictive float; the liquid level is in practice proportional to the bottom pressure. [S7]
- States: `normal`, `underRange`, `overRange`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `levelPercent` (number, %, M); `levelM` (number, m, M); `densityKgPerM3` (number, kg/m³, P).
- Model: level = bottom pressure / (density × g), then a first-order lag.

### Flow transmitter (transmetteur de débit)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `FT`. [S1]
- Real working: volume or mass flow, linked by density; differential pressure on a restriction, turbine, magnetic (conductive fluid), ultrasonic, Coriolis (direct mass flow), vortex; on an orifice, the pressure drop grows as the square of the flow. [S7]
- States: `normal`, `noFlow`, `overRange`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `flowRateM3PerH` (number, m³/h, M); `massFlowRateKgPerS` (number, kg/s, M).
- Model: orifice, `Q = k × √Δp`; then a first-order lag.

### Speed sensor (capteur de vitesse)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `ST`. [S1]
- Real working: an incremental encoder gives two quadrature signals: their frequency is the speed, the pulse count the distance, their phase the direction; a tachometer has one output, speed without direction. [S8]
- States: `normal`, `zeroSpeed`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `speedRpm` (number, rpm, M); `pulsesPerRevolutionCount` (integer, P); `hasWireBreakFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: speed = pulse frequency × 60 / pulses per revolution.

### Position transmitter (capteur de position)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `ZT`. [S1]
- Real working: an absolute encoder gives the position at power-on and keeps it; an incremental encoder gives only changes and needs a reference run; resolvers and potentiometers do the same in analog. [S8]
- States: `normal`, `notReferenced`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `positionPercent` (number, %, M); `angleDeg` (number, °, M); `isReferenced` (boolean, M).
- Model: position of the part, with an offset until the reference run for an incremental encoder.

## 4. Switches and loop (instrumentation catalog)

### Limit, pressure and level switch (contacts et seuils)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 bubble with `ZS`, `PS` or `LS`, plus `H` or `L`. [S1]
- Real working: a limit switch changes state when a part reaches a position; a pressure switch at a set pressure; a level switch tells if the liquid is above or below one point; wire a safety switch normally closed, so that a broken wire reads as "limit reached". [S7] [S8]
- States: `inactive`, `active`, plus `hasWireBreakFault`.
- Properties: `isActive` (boolean, M); `isNormallyClosed` (boolean, P); `switchSetpointBar`, `hysteresisBar` (number, bar, P, for a pressure switch); `hasWireBreakFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: active above the setpoint, inactive below the setpoint minus the hysteresis.

### 4 to 20 mA current loop (boucle 4-20 mA)

- Symbol: the signal line between the transmitter and the controller, on the project legend.
- Real working: 4 mA is 0 % and 20 mA is 100 % of the range; the live zero keeps the device powered by the loop; 0 mA means a broken circuit; under 3.8 mA or above 20.5 mA is a fault; HART carries digital data on the same loop. [S5]
- States: `normal`, `underRange`, `overRange`, `wireBreak`.
- Properties: `loopCurrentMa` (number, mA, M); `hasWireBreakFault` (boolean, D).
- Model: `loopCurrentMa = 4 + 16 × (value − rangeMin) / (rangeMax − rangeMin)`.

## 5. Alarm limits and health (instrumentation catalog)

### Alarm limits (seuils d'alarme)

- Symbol: ISA 5.1 tag letters `AHH`, `AH`, `AL`, `ALL` after the quantity letter. [S1]
- Real working: up to four limits (high-high, high, low, low-low), each with a deadband that delays the return to normal and a severity; limits are exclusive (one state at a time) or not. [S3]
- States: `normal`, `lowLow`, `low`, `high`, `highHigh`.
- Properties: `limitState` (enum, M); `highHighLimitBar`, `highLimitBar`, `lowLimitBar`, `lowLowLimitBar`, `alarmDeadbandBar` (number, bar, P, for a pressure); `isAlarmAcknowledged` (boolean, M); `isAcknowledgeCommanded` (boolean, C).
- Model: compare the value with each limit; return to normal only past the limit by the deadband.

### Device health (état de diagnostic)

- Symbol: a badge on the symbol, as `docs/synoptics/synoptic-rules.md` says.
- Real working: NAMUR NE 107 sorts device diagnostics in four signals: failure (invalid signal), function check (invalid for a time, during a test), maintenance required (works, near end of life), out of specification (outside allowed limits); a public device model adds `normal`. [S9] [S4]
- States: `normal`, `failure`, `checkFunction`, `offSpec`, `maintenanceRequired`.
- Properties: `health` (enum, M).
- Model: set by fault injection: wire break or stuck value gives `failure`, a test gives `checkFunction`.

## 6. Transmitter model (analog transmitter)

Model a transmitter as a first-order lag, `y = k / (T·s + 1) · u` [S10], then add offset, slow drift and random noise [S6].

```ts
interface PressureTransmitterModel {
  filteredPressureBar: number;
  biasOffsetBar: number;
  driftOffsetBar: number;
  noiseAmplitudeBar: number;
}

function stepTransmitter(model: PressureTransmitterModel, processBar: number, dampingS: number, driftRate: number, dtS: number): number {
  const alpha = dtS / (dampingS + dtS);
  model.filteredPressureBar += alpha * (processBar - model.filteredPressureBar);
  model.driftOffsetBar += driftRate * dtS;
  const noise = model.noiseAmplitudeBar * (Math.random() * 2 - 1);
  return model.filteredPressureBar + model.biasOffsetBar + model.driftOffsetBar + noise;
}
```

Clamp the result to the range, then compute the loop current. Faults to simulate: stuck value, wire break (`loopCurrentMa = 0`), out of range, `health = 'failure'`.

## Sources

- [S2] OPC Foundation, "OPC 30081 Process Automation Devices (PA-DIM)", sections 7.26 and 8.2, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-30081/8.2>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S3] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-9 Alarms and Conditions", section 5.8.18, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-9/5.8.18>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] OPC Foundation, "OPC 10000-100 Devices", section 4.5.4, <https://reference.opcfoundation.org/specs/OPC-10000-100/4.5.4>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Wikipedia, "Current loop", <https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Current_loop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY-SA 4.0, summarized.
