=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 34147d3b3f184cd00ff582cdac69736af89a606092bd36700bb50a35fca8932a RAW code/docs/typescript-lint/angular-components-v22.md
---
id: typescript-lint-angular-components-v22
title: Angular 22 components
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for inject(), signal inputs and outputs, lifecycle methods, host bindings and standalone components. Angular 22.
tool: angular-eslint
version: "22.6"
checked: 2026-11-13
sources: [S1, S2]
license: MIT, examples written for this base
variant: files
---

# Angular 22 components

Contents: 1. inject() · 2. Signal inputs · 3. Outputs · 4. Lifecycle methods · 5. Host bindings · 6. Output names · 7. Standalone · Sources

In Angular 22, components are standalone and use OnPush by default: never write `standalone: true`
and never write `changeDetection` unless the project asks for it. [S2]

## 1. Inject with inject(), not with the constructor (angular-eslint prefer-inject)

Get a service with a field: `private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);`. Never list services in the constructor. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Injectable({ providedIn: 'root' })
export class PumpStore {
  public constructor(private readonly http: HttpClient) {}
}
```

Right, with `@Service()`, the service decorator of Angular 22:

```ts
@Service()
export class PumpStore {
  private readonly http = inject(HttpClient);

  public load(): Observable<unknown> {
    return this.http.get<unknown>('/api/pumps');
  }
}
```

## 2. Use signal inputs (angular-eslint prefer-signals)

Declare an input with `input()` or `input.required()`, never with `@Input()`. Read it as a function in the template: `label()`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-label', template: '{{ label }}' })
export class PumpLabel {
  @Input() public label = '';
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-label', template: '{{ label() }}' })
export class PumpLabel {
  public readonly label = input('');
}
```

## 3. Use output() (angular-eslint prefer-output-emitter-ref)

Declare an output with `output<Type>()`, never with `@Output()` and `EventEmitter`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-stop-button',
  template: '<button type="button" (click)="stopped.emit()">Stop</button>',
})
export class StopButton {
  @Output() public readonly stopped = new EventEmitter<void>();
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-stop-button',
  template: '<button type="button" (click)="stopped.emit()">Stop</button>',
})
export class StopButton {
  public readonly stopped = output();
}
```

## 4. Keep lifecycle methods synchronous (angular-eslint no-async-lifecycle-method)

Never write `async ngOnInit()`. Angular does not wait for it. Start the work with `void`, in a separate method. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-valve-count', template: '{{ count() }}' })
export class ValveCount implements OnInit {
  protected readonly count = signal(0);
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async ngOnInit(): Promise<void> {
    this.count.set((await this.api.loadValves()).length);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-valve-count', template: '{{ count() }}' })
export class ValveCount implements OnInit {
  protected readonly count = signal(0);
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public ngOnInit(): void {
    void this.load();
  }

  private async load(): Promise<void> {
    const valves = await this.api.loadValves();

    this.count.set(valves.length);
  }
}
```

## 5. Bind host events in the metadata (angular-eslint prefer-host-metadata-property)

Write host events and bindings in `host: { … }` of `@Component`, never with `@HostListener` or `@HostBinding`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-escape-stop', template: '' })
export class EscapeStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();

  @HostListener('window:keydown.escape')
  protected stop(): void {
    this.stopped.emit();
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({
  selector: 'app-escape-stop',
  template: '',
  host: { '(window:keydown.escape)': 'stop()' },
})
export class EscapeStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();

  protected stop(): void {
    this.stopped.emit();
  }
}
```

## 6. Name an output after the event, without "on" (angular-eslint no-output-on-prefix)

Name an output `stopped` or `valveSelected`, never `onStop`. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-stop', template: '' })
export class PumpStop {
  public readonly onStop = output();
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-pump-stop', template: '' })
export class PumpStop {
  public readonly stopped = output();
}
```

## 7. Keep components standalone (angular-eslint prefer-standalone)

Never write `standalone: false`. Import what the template needs in `imports: [ … ]` of the component. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-tank-view', standalone: false, template: '' })
export class TankView {}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-tank-view', template: '' })
export class TankView {}
```

- [S1] angular-eslint, rules of @angular-eslint/eslint-plugin, version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/tree/main/packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "Angular coding style guide" and "Components", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/style-guide>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules to end subscriptions with the component and to turn streams into signals. Angular 22, RxJS 7.

# Angular and RxJS

Contents: 1. Streams as signals · 2. takeUntilDestroyed · 3. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Turn a stream into a signal (angular-eslint no-implicit-take-until-destroyed)

To show the last value of a stream, use `toSignal()` in a field. It ends the subscription with the component. [S2]

Why: `takeUntilDestroyed()` without an argument works only during the creation of the component. Called in `ngOnInit` or in a method, it throws an error.

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-banner', template: '{{ last() }}' })
export class AlarmBanner implements OnInit {
  protected readonly last = signal('');
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);

  public ngOnInit(): void {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe((message) => {
      this.last.set(message);
    });
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-banner', template: '{{ last() }}' })
export class AlarmBanner {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);
  protected readonly last = toSignal(this.alarms.messages$, { initialValue: '' });
}
```

## 2. Subscribe in a field when you must (angular-eslint no-implicit-take-until-destroyed)

When a stream must run code, subscribe while the component is created: in a field or the constructor, with `takeUntilDestroyed()`. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-sound', template: '' })
export class AlarmSound {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);

  public start(): void {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe(() => {
      this.ring();
    });
  }

  private ring(): void {
    this.rang.set(true);
  }

  private readonly rang = signal(false);
}
```

Right:

```ts
@Component({ selector: 'app-alarm-sound', template: '' })
export class AlarmSound {
  private readonly alarms = inject(AlarmFeed);
  private readonly rang = signal(false);

  public constructor() {
    this.alarms.messages$.pipe(takeUntilDestroyed()).subscribe(() => {
      this.rang.set(true);
    });
  }
}
```

## 3. Pitfalls (RxJS in Angular)

- A `subscribe()` inside another `subscribe()`: no lint rule catches it. Chain the streams with `switchMap` instead.
- `toPromise()` is deprecated in RxJS 7: use `firstValueFrom()` or `lastValueFrom()`.
- Keep state in signals; keep streams for events that happen over time (messages, timers, sockets).

## Sources

- [S1] angular-eslint, "no-implicit-take-until-destroyed", version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/blob/main/packages/eslint-plugin/docs/rules/no-implicit-take-until-destroyed.md>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

id: typescript-lint-angular-templates-v22
title: Angular 22 templates
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for control flow, class bindings, buttons, keyboard access and empty lists in templates. Angular 22.

# Angular 22 templates

Contents: 1. Control flow · 2. Class bindings · 3. Button type · 4. Click and keyboard · 5. Empty lists · Sources

## 1. Use @if and @for, never *ngIf and *ngFor (angular-eslint template prefer-control-flow)

Write `@if`, `@for` and `@switch` blocks. `*ngIf`, `*ngFor` and `*ngSwitch` are old. Each `@for` needs a `track` expression. [S1] [S2]

Wrong:

```html
<ul>
  <li *ngFor="let valve of valves">{{ valve.label }}</li>
</ul>
```

Right:

```html
<ul>
  @for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
    <li>{{ valve.label }}</li>
  }
</ul>
```

## 2. Bind one class at a time (angular-eslint template prefer-class-binding)

Write `[class.fault]="isFault()"`, never `[ngClass]`. Write `[style.width.px]`, never `[ngStyle]`. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
<li [ngClass]="{ fault: isFault() }">Valve</li>
```

Right:

```html
<li [class.fault]="isFault()">Valve</li>
```

## 3. Give every button its type (angular-eslint template button-has-type)

Write `type="button"` on each button that is not the submit button of a form. [S1]

Why: a button without type submits the form around it.

Wrong:

```html
<button (click)="stop()">Stop</button>
```

Right:

```html
<button type="button" (click)="stop()">Stop</button>
```

## 4. Put a click on a button, never on a plain element (angular-eslint template click-events-have-key-events)

Put `(click)` on a `<button>` or a link. A click on a `<li>` or a `<div>` cannot be reached with the keyboard. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
<li (click)="select()">Valve V-101</li>
```

Right:

```html
<li>
  <button type="button" (click)="select()">Valve V-101</button>
</li>
```

## 5. Show an empty list with @empty (angular-eslint template prefer-at-empty)

For the text of an empty list, write an `@empty` block inside `@for`, not a second `@if`. [S1]

Wrong:

```html
@for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
  <p>{{ valve.label }}</p>
}
@if (valves().length === 0) {
  <p>No valve</p>
}
```

Right:

```html
@for (valve of valves(); track valve.id) {
  <p>{{ valve.label }}</p>
} @empty {
  <p>No valve</p>
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] angular-eslint, rules of @angular-eslint/eslint-plugin-template, version 22.6, <https://github.com/angular-eslint/angular-eslint/tree/main/packages/eslint-plugin-template/docs/rules>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Google, "Control flow", Angular 22, <https://angular.dev/guide/templates/control-flow>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# Strict formatting

Contents: 1. Member order · 2. What the fix command does · 3. The switch · Sources

## 1. Write fields, then the constructor, then methods (typescript-eslint member-ordering)

In a class, write all fields first, then the constructor, then the methods. The fix command cannot move them for you. [S1]

Wrong:

```ts
export class TankGauge {
  public percent(level: number): number {
    return (level / this.capacity) * 100;
  }

  private readonly capacity = 40;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class TankGauge {
  private readonly capacity = 40;

  public percent(level: number): number {
    return (level / this.capacity) * 100;
  }
}
```

## 2. What the fix command orders for you (ESLint strict formatting)

`npm run fix` sorts imports and exports, adds braces to every `if`, adds a blank line before each `return` and between class members, and sorts the keys of Angular decorators. Prettier then sets spaces and line breaks. Never do these by hand: run the command. [S2]

## 3. The switch (STRICT_FORMAT)

The strict rules are on by default. `STRICT_FORMAT=0 npm run check` runs the check without them. Never turn them off to hide an error.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "member-ordering", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/member-ordering>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Rules of `eslint.strict-format.mjs` in `templates/typescript-angular/` of this base: ESLint core, `@stylistic/eslint-plugin` 5.10, `eslint-plugin-simple-import-sort` 14, angular-eslint 22.6, all MIT.

# TypeScript promises

Contents: 1. Handle every promise · 2. Await before you test · 3. async only with await · 4. Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Handle every promise (typescript-eslint no-floating-promises)

Handle each promise: `await` it, `return` it, or mark it with `void` when its result does not matter. [S1]

Why: a promise that nobody handles hides its error.

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveSaver {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public save(): void {
    this.router.navigate(['/valves']);
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveSaver {
  private readonly router = inject(Router);

  public async save(): Promise<void> {
    await this.router.navigate(['/valves']);
  }
}
```

For several promises, join them with `Promise.all()`, then handle the result. [S1]

## 2. Await a promise before you test it (typescript-eslint no-misused-promises)

Never put a promise in a condition. Await it first. [S2]

Why: a promise is always truthy, so the test is always true.

Wrong:

```ts
export class ValveCloser {
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async closeIfOpen(id: string): Promise<void> {
    if (this.api.isOpen(id)) {
      await this.api.close(id);
    }
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export class ValveCloser {
  private readonly api = inject(ValveApi);

  public async closeIfOpen(id: string): Promise<void> {
    if (await this.api.isOpen(id)) {
      await this.api.close(id);
    }
  }
}
```

## 3. Use async only with await (typescript-eslint require-await)

Mark a function `async` only when it contains `await`. [S3]

Why: an `async` function without `await` wraps a plain value in a promise for nothing.

Wrong:

```ts
export async function valveLabel(valve: Valve): Promise<string> {
  return valve.label;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function valveLabel(valve: Valve): string {
  return valve.label;
}
```

## 4. Pitfalls (typescript-eslint promise rules)

- `void` does not handle the error of the promise. Use `void` only when the error does not matter. Else use `await` inside `try`. [S1]
- An event handler of a template that calls an `async` method: write `void this.save()` in a method that returns `void`.
- Never add a comment to switch a promise rule off: such comments have no effect in this project.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "no-floating-promises", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-floating-promises>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-misused-promises", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-misused-promises>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "require-await", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/require-await>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

---
id: typescript-lint-ts-null-and-conditions
title: TypeScript null checks and conditions
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for index access, needless conditions, complete switch statements, template strings and nullish values. TypeScript 6.0.
tool: typescript-eslint, TypeScript

# TypeScript null checks and conditions

Contents: 1. Index access · 2. Needless conditions · 3. Complete switch · 4. Template strings · 5. Nullish values · Sources

## 1. A value read by its index can be undefined (TypeScript noUncheckedIndexedAccess)

With `noUncheckedIndexedAccess`, `list[0]` has the type `Item | undefined`. Check it before you use it. [S1]

Why: an empty array or a missing key gives `undefined`, not an item.

Wrong:

```ts
export function firstId(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves[0].id;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function firstId(valves: readonly Valve[]): string | undefined {
  return valves[0]?.id;
}
```

## 2. Test only what can change (typescript-eslint no-unnecessary-condition)

Never test a value that the types say is always set, or always true. Fix the type instead. [S2]

Why: a needless test hides a wrong type, and the other branch is dead code.

Wrong:

```ts
export function describeValve(valve: Valve): string {
  if (valve !== undefined) {
    return valve.label;
  }

  return 'unknown';
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function describeValve(valve: Valve | undefined): string {
  return valve?.label ?? 'unknown';
}
```

## 3. Handle every case of a union in a switch (typescript-eslint switch-exhaustiveness-check)

A `switch` on a union type has one `case` per member. Add no `default` that hides a new member. [S3]

Why: when a new state is added, the compiler then shows every switch to update.

Wrong:

```ts
export function stateText(state: ValveState): string {
  switch (state) {
    case 'open':
      return 'Open';
    case 'closed':
      return 'Closed';
    default:
      return '';
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function stateText(state: ValveState): string {
  switch (state) {
    case 'open':
      return 'Open';
    case 'closed':
      return 'Closed';
    case 'fault':
      return 'Fault';
  }
}
```

## 4. Put only strings and numbers in a template string (typescript-eslint restrict-template-expressions)

In `${…}`, put a string or a number. Give a fallback for a value that can be `undefined`. [S4]

Why: `${undefined}` prints the word "undefined" on the screen.

Wrong:

```ts
export function tankLine(tank: string | undefined, percent: number): string {
  return `${tank} ${percent} %`;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function tankLine(tank: string | undefined, percent: number): string {
  return `${tank ?? 'unknown tank'} ${percent} %`;
}
```

## 5. Use ?? for a missing value (typescript-eslint prefer-nullish-coalescing)

Use `??` to replace `undefined` or `null`. Use `||` only to replace every false value on purpose. [S5]

Why: `||` also replaces `0` and the empty string, which are real values.

Wrong:

```ts
export function labelOf(label: string | undefined): string {
  return label || 'none';
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function labelOf(label: string | undefined): string {
  return label ?? 'none';
}
```

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TSConfig Reference: noUncheckedIndexedAccess", TypeScript 6.0, <https://www.typescriptlang.org/tsconfig/#noUncheckedIndexedAccess>, read 2026-11-13, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-unnecessary-condition", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unnecessary-condition>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "switch-exhaustiveness-check", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/switch-exhaustiveness-check>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] typescript-eslint, "restrict-template-expressions", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/restrict-template-expressions>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S5] typescript-eslint, "prefer-nullish-coalescing", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/prefer-nullish-coalescing>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

---
id: typescript-lint-ts-types
title: TypeScript types without any
topic: typescript-lint
summary: Rules for any, unknown, type assertions, non-null assertions and thrown values, with examples. typescript-eslint 8.
tool: typescript-eslint
version: "8.72"

# TypeScript types without any

Contents: 1. unknown, never any · 2. Check the shape · 3. Never write ! · 4. Throw an Error · Pitfalls · Sources

## 1. Use unknown, never any (typescript-eslint no-explicit-any)

Never write `any`. Write `unknown`, then check the value before you use it. [S1]

Why: `any` turns the type checks off, so every later error goes unseen.

Wrong:

```ts
export function readLevel(sample: any): number {
  return sample.level;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function readLevel(sample: unknown): number {
  if (typeof sample === 'object' && sample !== null && 'level' in sample) {
    return Number(sample.level);
  }

  return 0;
}
```

## 2. Check the shape, never force a type (typescript-eslint no-unsafe-type-assertion)

Never write `as <Type>` to give a value a narrower type. Check its shape with a type guard. [S2]

Why: `as` tells the compiler to trust you. A wrong shape then breaks at run time, far away.

Wrong:

```ts
export function toValves(body: unknown): Valve[] {
  return body as Valve[];
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function toValves(body: unknown): Valve[] {
  if (!Array.isArray(body) || !body.every(isValve)) {
    throw new Error('The valve list has a wrong shape.');
  }

  return body;
}
```

## 3. Check for undefined, never write ! (typescript-eslint no-non-null-assertion)

Never write `!` after a value. Use `?.` and `??`, or test for `undefined`. [S3]

Why: `!` hides a value that can be missing. The code then fails on the first missing value.

Wrong:

```ts
export function firstOpenLabel(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves.find((valve) => valve.state === 'open')!.label;
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function firstOpenLabel(valves: readonly Valve[]): string {
  return valves.find((valve) => valve.state === 'open')?.label ?? 'none';
}
```

## 4. Throw an Error object (typescript-eslint only-throw-error)

Throw only `Error` objects: `throw new Error('message')`. [S4]

Why: a thrown string has no stack trace, and `catch` code expects an `Error`.

Wrong:

```ts
export function requireOpen(valve: Valve): void {
  if (valve.state !== 'open') {
    throw 'The valve is closed.';
  }
}
```

Right:

```ts
export function requireOpen(valve: Valve): void {
  if (valve.state !== 'open') {
    throw new Error('The valve is closed.');
  }
}
```

## 5. Pitfalls (typescript-eslint unsafe rules)

- `Unsafe member access .x on an any value` or `Unsafe return of a value of type any`: an `any` came in earlier. Find where it came from and type it as `unknown` there.
- A value in `catch (error)` has the type `unknown`. Check it with `error instanceof Error` before you read `error.message`.
- `JSON.parse` and an HTTP body give an untyped value: read them as `unknown`, then check them.

## Sources

- [S1] typescript-eslint, "no-explicit-any", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-explicit-any>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] typescript-eslint, "no-unsafe-type-assertion", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-unsafe-type-assertion>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S3] typescript-eslint, "no-non-null-assertion", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/no-non-null-assertion>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] typescript-eslint, "only-throw-error", version 8, <https://typescript-eslint.io/rules/only-throw-error>, read 2026-11-13, MIT, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 a929bde467566ce5b7502440c2af3f9b33d79ef149d651322e1dd3ee480eca0d RAW code/docs/typescript/index.md

# Node.js versions and built-in APIs

Contents: 1. Versions · 2. Running a TypeScript file · 3. Built-in APIs · Sources

## 1. Versions (Node.js LTS)

| Node.js | Released | State on 2026-11-12 | Angular that accepts it |
| --- | --- | --- | --- |
| 26 | 2026-05-05 | LTS | 22 |
| 24 | 2025-05-06 | LTS | 20, 21; 22 from 24.15 |
| 22 | 2024-04-24 | LTS | 18 to 21; 22 from 22.22.3 |
| 20 | 2023-04-17 | End of life | 17 to 21 |

Use only LTS versions in production. From version 27, one major version a year, and every version becomes LTS. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Running a TypeScript file directly (Node.js type stripping)

From Node.js 22.18, `node file.ts` runs a TypeScript file with no option. Node.js replaces the types by spaces: it checks no type and ignores `tsconfig.json`. [S3]

- Refused: `enum`, a `namespace` with code, parameter properties in a constructor, decorators, `.tsx` files.
- Write the extension in each import: `import { x } from './file.ts'`. Write `import type` for a type.
- A `.ts` file under `node_modules` is refused.

Wrong:

```ts
enum Mode { Fix, Check }
import { checkBase } from './check-base';
```

Right:

```ts
const MODES = ['fix', 'check'] as const;
import { checkBase } from './check-base.ts';
```

## 3. Built-in APIs that replace packages (Node.js 22 to 26)

| API | Since | Replaces |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `node --watch` | 22, stable | A tool that restarts on change |
| `glob`, `globSync` in `node:fs` | 22 | A file pattern package |
| `WebSocket` (client) | 22 | A WebSocket client package |
| `fetch`, `util.parseArgs`, `node:test` and `node --test` | 22 at most: the scripts and tests of this base use them with 22.18 | An HTTP client, an argument parser, a test runner |
| `require()` of an ES module | 22.12 | CommonJS and ES module workarounds |
| `Temporal` | 26 | A date library |

[S4] [S2] [S5]

## Sources

- [S1] Node.js, "Releases", <https://nodejs.org/en/about/previous-releases>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Node.js, "Modules: TypeScript", v26, <https://nodejs.org/api/typescript.html>, read 2026-11-12, MIT, summarized.
- [S4] Node.js, "Node.js 22 is now available!", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/announcements/v22-release-announce>, and "Node.js 26.0.0", <https://nodejs.org/en/blog/release/v26.0.0>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S5] Scripts and tests of this base, run with Node.js 22.18.0 and 24.18.0 on 2026-11-13.

# RxJS 7 and Angular signals

Contents: 1. RxJS stays at 7 · 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 · 3. Signals and observables · 4. The bridge · Sources

## 1. RxJS stays at 7 (RxJS versions)

The current version is 7.8.2. Version 8 is paused, with no release date: write RxJS 7 code. Angular 16 to 22 accept `^6.5.3 || ^7.4.0`. [S1] [S2]

## 2. Deprecated in RxJS 7 (RxJS 7 deprecations)

| Old pattern | Write instead |
| --- | --- |
| `source$.toPromise()` | `await firstValueFrom(source$)` or `await lastValueFrom(source$)` |
| `subscribe(next, error, complete)` | `subscribe({ next, error, complete })` |
| `Observable.create(…)` | `new Observable(…)` |

[S1]

Wrong:

```ts
const valves = await this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves').toPromise();
```

Right:

```ts
const valves = await firstValueFrom(this.http.get<unknown>('/api/valves'));
```

## 3. Signals for state, observables for streams (Angular signals and RxJS)

- State of a component, and values derived from it: signals (`signal`, `computed`). [S3]
- Events over time, cancellation, combination of streams (messages, timers, sockets): observables. [S3]
- An observable in a template: the `async` pipe, or `toSignal()` in the component.
- A simple HTTP request, from Angular 22: `httpResource()`. Before 22: `HttpClient`.

## 4. The bridge between signals and observables (rxjs-interop)

| API, from `@angular/core/rxjs-interop` | Role | To know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `toSignal(obs$, { initialValue })` | Observable to signal | Subscribes at once; needs the injection context, so call it in a field; without `initialValue`, gives `undefined` until the first value |
| `toObservable(signal)` | Signal to observable | Several synchronous writes give one value only |
| `rxResource({ params, stream })` | Resource fed by an observable | Same state signals as `resource` |
| `takeUntilDestroyed()` | End a subscription with the component | Without an argument, only in a field or the constructor |

[S3]

## Sources

- [S1] ReactiveX, types of `rxjs` 7.8.2 (`@deprecated` tags), <https://app.unpkg.com/rxjs@7.8.2/files/dist/types/internal/Observable.d.ts>, and the roadmap issue 6367, <https://github.com/ReactiveX/rxjs/issues/6367>, read 2026-11-12, Apache 2.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] Angular Team, "RxJS interop with Angular signals", v22, <https://angular.dev/ecosystem/rxjs-interop>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.

# TypeScript 5.0 to 7.0: what changed

Contents: 1. Version rule · 2. Syntax from 5.0 to 5.9 · 3. TypeScript 6.0 · 4. TypeScript 7.0 · Sources

## 1. Version rule (TypeScript versions)

Read the version of `typescript` in `package.json`. Use a syntax only when that version is equal to or higher than the version that added it. [S1]

In an Angular 22 project, `typescript` stays at 6.0: Angular 22 and typescript-eslint 8 accept `>=6.0.0 <6.1.0`. Never move it to 7 when you update dependencies. [S2] [S4]

## 2. Syntax added from 5.0 to 5.9 (TypeScript 5)

| Version | Use | Breaking change to know |
| --- | --- | --- |
| 5.0 | Standard decorators; `const` type parameters; `extends` with an array in `tsconfig`; `moduleResolution: bundler`; `verbatimModuleSyntax`; `export type *` | `target: ES3`, `out`, `importsNotUsedAsValues` deprecated |
| 5.2 | `using` and `await using` for disposable resources | `module` and `moduleResolution` must match for `node16` and `nodenext` |
| 5.3 | Import attributes: `import data from './a.json' with { type: 'json' }` | `super.x` on an instance field is an error |
| 5.4 | `NoInfer<T>`; narrowing kept in closures; `Object.groupBy`; `module: preserve` | Stricter assignment between enums |
| 5.5 | Inferred type predicates: `values.filter((v) => v !== undefined)` narrows the type | Options deprecated in 5.0 have no effect |
| 5.6 | Conditions always true or always false are errors; iterator helper methods | `.tsbuildinfo` always written |
| 5.8 | `erasableSyntaxOnly`: only syntax that Node.js can strip | `assert` in an import is refused under `nodenext` |
| 5.9 | `import defer`; `module: node20` | `ArrayBuffer` is no longer a supertype of `Buffer` |

[S1]

## 3. TypeScript 6.0 (released 2026-03-23)

New defaults: `strict: true`, `module: esnext`, `target` the latest ECMAScript (`es2025`), `types: []`, `rootDir` the folder of `tsconfig.json`, `noUncheckedSideEffectImports: true`. [S3]

| Old option or syntax | State in 6.0 | Write instead |
| --- | --- | --- |
| `"moduleResolution": "node"` or `"node10"` | Deprecated | `bundler` or `nodenext` |
| `"moduleResolution": "classic"`, `"outFile"` | Removed | `bundler`; ES modules |
| `"baseUrl"` | Deprecated | Full paths in `paths` |
| `"target": "es5"`, `"downlevelIteration"` | Deprecated | ES2015 at least |
| `"module": "amd"`, `"umd"`, `"systemjs"`, `"none"` | Not supported | ES modules |
| `module Foo { }` | Error | `namespace Foo { }` |
| `import x from './a.json' assert { type: 'json' }` | Error | `with { type: 'json' }` |

- To silence the deprecations while you migrate: `"ignoreDeprecations": "6.0"`. Remove it once done. [S3]
- New types: `Temporal`, `Map.prototype.getOrInsert`, `RegExp.escape`. [S3]

## 4. TypeScript 7.0 (released 2026-07-08)

- The compiler is native: builds are 7 to 12 times faster. `npm install -D typescript` installs it. [S4]
- It has no programming API before 7.1: Angular and typescript-eslint cannot use it yet. [S4]
- The options deprecated in 6.0 are errors. [S4]
- The 6.0 compiler stays available as `@typescript/typescript6`, command `tsc6`. [S4]

## Sources

- [S1] Microsoft, "TypeScript 5.0" to "TypeScript 5.9" release notes, <https://typescriptlang.org/docs/handbook/release-notes/typescript-5-0.html> and the pages of the following versions, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S2] Angular Team, "Version compatibility", v22, <https://angular.dev/reference/versions>, read 2026-11-12, CC BY 4.0, summarized.
- [S3] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 6.0", 2026-03-23, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-6-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.
- [S4] D. Rosenwasser, "Announcing TypeScript 7.0", 2026-07-08, <https://devblogs.microsoft.com/typescript/announcing-typescript-7-0/>, read 2026-11-12, summarized.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 e81e183fa175c164d8406eeb85550be0265740ffc11a52744c3bf53b976b0c84 RAW code/templates/containers/angular.Dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of an Angular application served as static files. Copy as Dockerfile next to nginx.conf,
# replace "web" with the project name of angular.json, and set the versions of the project.

ARG NODE_VERSION=24
ARG NGINX_VERSION=1.29

FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /app
COPY package.json package-lock.json ./
RUN npm ci
COPY . .
RUN npm run build

FROM docker.io/nginx/nginx-unprivileged:${NGINX_VERSION} AS final
COPY nginx.conf /etc/nginx/conf.d/default.conf
COPY --from=build /app/dist/web/browser /usr/share/nginx/html
# The unprivileged user of the NGINX image (UID 101).
USER 101
EXPOSE 8080

# Deployment and Service of an API. Replace the name "api", the image, the resources and the probe
# timings with the values of the project.
apiVersion: apps/v1
kind: Deployment
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  replicas: 2
  selector:
    matchLabels:
      app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  strategy:
    type: RollingUpdate
    rollingUpdate:
      maxUnavailable: 0
      maxSurge: 1
  template:
    metadata:
      labels:
        app.kubernetes.io/name: api
    spec:
      securityContext:
        runAsNonRoot: true
        seccompProfile:
          type: RuntimeDefault
      imagePullSecrets:
        - name: registry-pull
      containers:
        - name: api
          image: registry.example.internal/api:1.0.0
          ports:
            - containerPort: 8080
          startupProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/live
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 30
          livenessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/live
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 10
            failureThreshold: 3
          readinessProbe:
            httpGet:
              path: /healthz/ready
              port: 8080
            periodSeconds: 5
            failureThreshold: 3
          resources:
            requests:
              cpu: "1"
              memory: "512Mi"
            limits:
              cpu: "1"
              memory: "512Mi"
          securityContext:
            allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
            readOnlyRootFilesystem: true
            capabilities:
              drop: ["ALL"]
          volumeMounts:
            - name: tmp
              mountPath: /tmp
      volumes:
        - name: tmp
          emptyDir: {}
---
apiVersion: v1
kind: Service
metadata:
  name: api
  labels:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
spec:
  selector:
    app.kubernetes.io/name: api
  ports:
    - port: 80
      targetPort: 8080

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 754daa071462ae994ddbe85d89887790013a6c5ef01306085d505be932004cc8 RAW code/templates/containers/devcontainer/Dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of the .NET and Angular dev container. The coding assistant and the check tools run in it.
# Copy this folder as .devcontainer/ at the project root.

ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0
ARG NODE_VERSION=24

FROM docker.io/library/node:${NODE_VERSION} AS node

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION}

# Node.js and npm, taken from the official Node.js image.
COPY --from=node /usr/local/bin/node /usr/local/bin/
COPY --from=node /usr/local/lib/node_modules /usr/local/lib/node_modules
RUN ln -s ../lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npm-cli.js /usr/local/bin/npm \
 && ln -s ../lib/node_modules/npm/bin/npx-cli.js /usr/local/bin/npx

# Check tools: put the Linux binaries of hadolint, kubeconform, kube-linter and helm in tools/ once.
COPY tools/ /usr/local/bin/

# Kubernetes JSON schemas for kubeconform, for the version of the cluster.
COPY kubernetes-schemas/ /opt/kubernetes-schemas/

# The coding assistant: its npm package with its version, "<package>@<version>", set in devcontainer.json.
ARG ASSISTANT_PACKAGE=""
# hadolint ignore=DL3016
RUN if [ -n "$ASSISTANT_PACKAGE" ]; then npm install --global "$ASSISTANT_PACKAGE"; fi

# Tools of the skill read-heavy-file: Poppler for PDF files, Tesseract for OCR in English and
# French. Set EXTRACT_TOOLS to false in devcontainer.json when apt packages cannot be installed.
ARG EXTRACT_TOOLS=true
# hadolint ignore=DL3008
RUN if [ "$EXTRACT_TOOLS" = "true" ]; then \
      apt-get update \
      && apt-get install --yes --no-install-recommends \
        poppler-utils tesseract-ocr tesseract-ocr-eng tesseract-ocr-fra \
      && rm -rf /var/lib/apt/lists/*; \
    fi

# Non-root user of the .NET images (UID 1654).
USER app

{
  // .NET and Angular dev container. The coding assistant runs inside it, so the check commands
  // of AGENTS.md are plain terminal commands.
  "name": "dotnet-angular",
  "build": {
    "dockerfile": "Dockerfile",
    "context": ".",
    "args": {
      "DOTNET_VERSION": "10.0",
      "NODE_VERSION": "24",
      // The npm package of the coding assistant, with its version: "<package>@<version>".
      "ASSISTANT_PACKAGE": "",
      // "false" when apt packages cannot be installed: no PDF text extraction and no OCR then.
      "EXTRACT_TOOLS": "true"
    }
  },
  "remoteUser": "app",
  "forwardPorts": [
    8080,
    4200
  ],
  // Once, after creation: fail early when a check tool is missing from the image.
  // PDF and OCR tools are optional: only a message when they are missing.
  "onCreateCommand": "hadolint --version && kubeconform -v && kube-linter version && helm version && { command -v pdftotext && command -v tesseract || echo 'No PDF or OCR tools: EXTRACT_TOOLS is false.'; }",
  // Restore before any check: the tool waits for this command.
  "updateContentCommand": {
    "dotnet": "dotnet restore && dotnet tool restore",
    "npm": "npm ci"
  },
  "postStartCommand": "dotnet --version && node --version"
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 1a7d6b05c80c562808326f48875de663a4f1653eb6a15e820f91be31fc736912 RAW code/templates/containers/dotnet.Dockerfile
# syntax=docker/dockerfile:1
# check=error=true
# Image of an ASP.NET Core API. Copy as Dockerfile, then replace "Api" with the project name
# and set DOTNET_VERSION from TargetFramework (net10.0 gives 10.0).

ARG DOTNET_VERSION=10.0

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/sdk:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS build
WORKDIR /source

# Copy the project file first: the restore layer stays cached while it does not change.
COPY Api/Api.csproj Api/
RUN dotnet restore Api/Api.csproj

# Copy the rest of the source, then publish.
COPY Api/ Api/
RUN dotnet publish Api/Api.csproj -c Release -o /app --no-restore

FROM mcr.microsoft.com/dotnet/aspnet:${DOTNET_VERSION} AS final
WORKDIR /app
COPY --from=build /app ./
USER $APP_UID
EXPOSE 8080
ENTRYPOINT ["dotnet", "Api.dll"]

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 6213607f9616b801b9a35b8cee52c59f3ceac2065852680869e47864fc80941f RAW code/templates/containers/nginx.conf
# Static server of an Angular application, unprivileged NGINX on port 8080.
server {
  listen 8080;
  root /usr/share/nginx/html;
  index index.html;

  # Every unknown route returns index.html: Angular routing handles it.
  location / {
    try_files $uri $uri/ /index.html;
  }
}

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 f1ca2479dc0d7180411b94d91465daa05aee3bcbedfe194e089cff6b64c44862 RAW code/templates/csharp/format-csharp.sh
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Format C# code, or check its formatting with --check.
# One line of Directory.Build.props selects the formatter:
#   <StrictFormatting>true</StrictFormatting>   CSharpier, with code-style and analyzer fixes
#   <StrictFormatting>false</StrictFormatting>  dotnet format
# Run it from the folder that holds Directory.Build.props and the project or solution file.
# Strict mode needs CSharpier once: dotnet new tool-manifest; dotnet tool install csharpier
set -euo pipefail

mode="fix"
if [[ "${1:-}" == "--check" ]]; then
  mode="check"
fi

strict="false"
if grep -q "<StrictFormatting>true</StrictFormatting>" Directory.Build.props; then
  strict="true"
fi

if [[ "$strict" == "true" && "$mode" == "fix" ]]; then
  dotnet format style --no-restore
  dotnet format analyzers --no-restore
  dotnet csharpier format .
elif [[ "$strict" == "true" ]]; then
  dotnet csharpier check .
  dotnet format style --no-restore --verify-no-changes
  dotnet format analyzers --no-restore --verify-no-changes
elif [[ "$mode" == "fix" ]]; then
  dotnet format --no-restore
else
  dotnet format --no-restore --verify-no-changes
fi
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== END

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 0db5d88d059b77ea8b45645deb2d5023023331961daf3c901e855099f7705334 RAW code/templates/typescript-angular/synoptic-symbol.ts
// A reusable synoptic symbol, as docs/synoptics/symbol-component.md describes: a pure function
// turns the inputs into data attributes, one state word and one accessible name. The styles pick
// the fill and the badge from the data attributes; one clock per view makes the badges flash.
export type PositionState = 'open' | 'closed' | 'opening' | 'closing' | 'intermediate' | 'unknown';
export type AlarmPriority = 'none' | 'caution' | 'warning' | 'alarm' | 'emergency';
export type AlarmState = 'normal' | 'unacknowledged' | 'acknowledged';

export interface ValveSymbolInputs {
  readonly tagName: string;
  readonly positionState: PositionState;
  readonly actualPositionPercent?: number;
  readonly alarmPriority: AlarmPriority;
  readonly alarmState: AlarmState;
  readonly isOutOfService: boolean;
}

export interface SymbolView {
  readonly attributes: Readonly<Record<string, string>>;
  readonly stateText: string;
  readonly badgeLetter: string;
  readonly isBadgeFlashing: boolean;
  readonly accessibleName: string;
}

const POSITION_TEXT: Readonly<Record<PositionState, string>> = {
  open: 'OPEN',
  closed: 'CLOSED',
  opening: 'OPENING',
  closing: 'CLOSING',
  intermediate: 'PARTIAL',
  unknown: '?',
};
const BADGE_LETTER: Readonly<Record<AlarmPriority, string>> = {
  none: '',
  caution: 'C',
  warning: 'W',
  alarm: 'A',
  emergency: 'E',
};

function stateTextOf(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): string {
  if (inputs.positionState === 'intermediate' && inputs.actualPositionPercent !== undefined) {
    return `${String(Math.round(inputs.actualPositionPercent))} %`;
  }
  return POSITION_TEXT[inputs.positionState];
}

export function toValveView(inputs: ValveSymbolInputs): SymbolView {
  const stateText = stateTextOf(inputs);
  const hasAlert = inputs.alarmPriority !== 'none';
  const alarmText = hasAlert ? `, ${inputs.alarmPriority} ${inputs.alarmState}` : '';

  return {
    attributes: {
      'data-position': inputs.positionState,
      'data-alarm-priority': inputs.alarmPriority,
      'data-alarm-state': inputs.alarmState,
      'data-out-of-service': String(inputs.isOutOfService),
    },
    stateText,
    badgeLetter: BADGE_LETTER[inputs.alarmPriority],
    isBadgeFlashing: hasAlert && inputs.alarmState === 'unacknowledged',
    accessibleName: `Valve ${inputs.tagName}, ${stateText}${alarmText}`,
  };
}

// One clock per view: it sets data-flash-phase on the view root, so that every badge flashes in
// phase, at 1 Hz by default. Call the returned function when the view closes.
export function startFlashClock(root: HTMLElement, periodMs = 1000): () => void {
  if (window.matchMedia('(prefers-reduced-motion: reduce)').matches) {
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = 'on';
    return () => undefined;
  }
  let isOn = true;
  const timer = window.setInterval(() => {
    isOn = !isOn;
    root.dataset['flashPhase'] = isOn ? 'on' : 'off';
  }, periodMs / 2);

  return () => {
    window.clearInterval(timer);
  };
}
=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== END

# Commit assistant

You are the commit message assistant of a team. Some users are not developers: use plain words.

## Rules

1. If the request is not about a commit message, refuse in one sentence and say what you can do.
2. You can do three things: propose a commit message, explain the type you chose, fix a message the user pastes.
3. Ask what changed and why, if the user did not say it. Ask one short question at a time.
4. Follow the skill write-commit-message for the format of the message.
5. Write the commit message in English. Talk to the user in the language of the question, never a mix of two languages.
6. Never invent a change. Use only what the user says.
7. You cannot run commands or make the commit. Never say that you did. The user copies the message.
8. Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Answer format

1. The commit message in a `text` code block.
2. One sentence: the type you chose and why.
3. If the change does two things, two messages, one per commit.

3. Name each document you used on a last line that starts with `Sources:`.
4. For a piece of equipment, explain its real working first, then its model: inputs, outputs, states, law.
5. Give property names from the catalogs and the naming convention of your knowledge. Never invent a unit suffix.
6. Give each equipment term in English, with the French term in parentheses: "valve (vanne)".
7. Answer in the language of the question, never a mix of two languages. Write code and names in English.
8. Use public knowledge only. Say when a value is historical or comes from a single source.
9. Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Answer format

1. The answer in short sections: real working, model, properties.
2. Properties in a table: name, type, unit, role.
3. The `Sources:` line.

# TypeScript and Angular

You help developers write and fix TypeScript and Angular code.

## Rules

1. Before you answer, search your knowledge for the subject of the question. Answer from what you find.
2. If your knowledge has nothing on the question, say so in one sentence, then answer from what you know and say that it is not from the knowledge.
3. Name each document you used on a last line that starts with `Sources:`.
4. Ask for the versions of `@angular/core` and `typescript` in `package.json` when the answer depends on them. Write only what these versions allow.
5. Write TypeScript, never plain JavaScript.
6. Follow the "Right" examples of the lint rules. Never hide an error with a comment that turns a rule off.
7. Answer in the language of the question, never a mix of two languages. Write code, names and comments in English.
8. You cannot read files or run commands. Give the commands for the user to run, and ask for their output.
9. Never search the web. Never open a URL.

## Answer format

1. The answer in a few short sentences or a short list.
2. The code in a `ts` or `html` block, complete enough to compile.
3. The commands to check it, when there is code: `npm run fix`, `npm run check`, `npm run build`.
4. The `Sources:` line.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 25789aeb36db87c31dd15b7a2abbdc30d57eada069d27b3f78d1f5cddc5f3aed RAW adapters/mcp/docs-server.ts
// Docs server of the knowledge base, for a coding assistant that speaks MCP over stdio.
// Run with Node 22.18 or later, no build step, no package:
//   node adapters/mcp/docs-server.ts [--root <folder>]
// The root holds docs/: the project root by default, or code/ of this repository.
// It speaks the protocol revision 2026-07-28 (request metadata, server/discover) and the
// revisions up to 2025-11-25 (initialize handshake). It never writes a file.
import {
  existsSync,
  readdirSync,
  readFileSync,
  realpathSync,
  statSync,
} from 'node:fs';
import {
  join,
  relative,
  resolve,
  sep,
} from 'node:path';
import { createInterface } from 'node:readline';
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';
import { parseArgs } from 'node:util';

type Json = Record<string, unknown>;

interface Request {
  id?: number | string | null;
  method?: string;
  params?: Json;
}

interface Entry {
  path: string;
  section?: string;
  text: string;
}

interface ToolResult {
  text: string;
  isError: boolean;
}

export const MODERN_VERSION = '2026-07-28';

export const LEGACY_VERSIONS = [
  '2025-11-25',
  '2025-06-18',
  '2025-03-26',
  '2024-11-05',
];

export const MAX_RESULTS = 10;
export const MAX_CHARS = 16_000;
const VERSION_KEY = 'io.modelcontextprotocol/protocolVersion';
const SERVER_INFO = {
  name: 'knowledge-base-docs',
  version: '0.1.0',
};
const INSTRUCTIONS = 'Find documents of the knowledge base with search_docs. Then read one section with read_doc. Read the table of contents of a document before a section.';
const INDEX_LINE = /^- \[([^\]]+)\]\((docs\/[^)]+)\): (.+)$/;
const DOC_PATH = /^docs\/[\w-]+(?:\/[\w.-]+)*\.md$/;
const NEWLINE = '\n';
const TOOLS = [
  {
    name: 'search_docs',
    title: 'Search the knowledge base',
    description: 'Search the topic indexes and the section titles of the knowledge base. Returns at most 10 lines: a document path, or a path and a section title.',
    inputSchema: {
      type: 'object',
      properties: {
        query: {
          type: 'string',
          description: 'English words, for example "valve flow coefficient".',
        },
      },
      required: ['query'],
      additionalProperties: false,
    },
  },
  {
    name: 'read_doc',
    title: 'Read one section of a document',
    description: 'Read one section of a document of the knowledge base. Without a section, returns the summary and the table of contents of the document.',
    inputSchema: {
      type: 'object',
      properties: {
        path: {
          type: 'string',
          description: 'Path of the document, as search_docs gives it, for example "docs/simulation/principles.md".',
        },
        section: {
          type: 'string',
          description: 'Number or title of the section, as the table of contents gives it.',
        },
      },
      required: ['path'],
      additionalProperties: false,
    },
  },
];

export function resolveDocsRoot(root: string): string {
  if (!existsSync(join(root, 'docs')) && existsSync(join(root, 'code', 'docs'))) {
    return join(root, 'code');
  }
  return root;
}

function read(path: string): string {
  return readFileSync(path, 'utf8');
}

function topicIndexes(root: string): string[] {
  const docs = join(root, 'docs');

  if (!existsSync(docs)) {
    return [];
  }
  return readdirSync(docs)
    .filter((topic) => statSync(join(docs, topic)).isDirectory())
    .toSorted()
    .map((topic) => join(docs, topic, 'index.md'))
    .filter((path) => existsSync(path));
}

function headings(text: string): string[] {
  return text
    .split(NEWLINE)
    .filter((line) => line.startsWith('## '))
    .map((line) => line.slice(3).trim());
}

function entries(root: string): Entry[] {
  return topicIndexes(root).flatMap((index) => read(index)
    .split(NEWLINE)
    .flatMap((line) => {
      const match = INDEX_LINE.exec(line.trim());

      if (match === null) {
        return [];
      }
      const [
        ,
        title,
        path,
        summary,
      ] = match;
      const file = join(root, path);
      const sections = existsSync(file) ? headings(read(file)) : [];

      return [
        {
          path,
          text: `${title}: ${summary}`,
        },
        ...sections.map((section) => ({
          path,
          section,
          text: `${title} ${section}`,
        })),
      ];
    }));
}

function words(text: string): string[] {
  return text
    .toLowerCase()
    .split(/[^a-z\d]+/)
    .filter((word) => word.length >= 2);
}

export function searchDocs(root: string, query: string): ToolResult {
  const asked = [...new Set(words(query))];

  if (asked.length === 0) {
    return {
      text: 'Give at least one English word to search.',
      isError: true,
    };
  }
  const found = entries(root)
    .map((entry) => {
      const known = new Set(words(entry.text));

      return {
        entry,
        score: asked.filter((word) => known.has(word)).length,
      };
    })
    .filter((item) => item.score > 0)
    .toSorted((first, second) => second.score - first.score)
    .slice(0, MAX_RESULTS)
    .map(({ entry }) => entry.section === undefined ? `${entry.path} | ${entry.text}` : `${entry.path} | section: ${entry.section}`);

  return {
    text: found.length === 0 ? `No document matches "${query}". Try other English words.` : found.join(NEWLINE),
    isError: false,
  };
}

function safeDocument(root: string, path: string): string | undefined {
  if (!DOC_PATH.test(path) || path.endsWith('.fr.md') || path
    .split('/')
    .includes('..')) {
    return undefined;
  }
  const docs = resolve(root, 'docs');
  const file = resolve(root, path);

  return relative(docs, file).startsWith(`..${sep}`) || !existsSync(file) ? undefined : file;
}

function frontField(text: string, name: string): string {
  const match = new RegExp(`^${name}: (.+)$`, 'm').exec(text.split(/^---$/m)[1] ?? '');

  return match?.[1]?.trim() ?? '';
}

function tableOfContents(path: string, text: string): string {
  return [
    `${path}: ${frontField(text, 'title')}`,
    `Summary: ${frontField(text, 'summary')}`,
    'Sections:',
    ...headings(text).map((title, number) => /^\d+\. /.test(title) ? title : `${number + 1}. ${title}`),
    'Read one section with read_doc and its number or title.',
  ].join(NEWLINE);
}

function sectionBody(text: string, title: string): string {
  const lines = text.split(NEWLINE);
  const start = lines.indexOf(`## ${title}`);
  const end = lines.findIndex((line, index) => index > start && line.startsWith('## '));

  return lines
    .slice(start, end === -1 ? undefined : end)
    .join(NEWLINE)
    .trim();
}

function findSection(titles: string[], asked: string): string | undefined {
  const wanted = asked
    .trim()
    .toLowerCase();
  const number = Number.parseInt(wanted, 10);

  if (String(number) === wanted) {
    return titles[number - 1];
  }
  return titles.find((title) => title.toLowerCase() === wanted) ?? titles.find((title) => title.toLowerCase().includes(wanted));
}

// The sources a section cites, taken from the Sources section of its document.
function citedSources(text: string, body: string): string[] {
  const cited = new Set(body.match(/\[S\d+\]/g) ?? []);

  return sectionBody(text, 'Sources')
    .split(NEWLINE)
    .filter((line) => [...cited].some((tag) => line.includes(tag)));
}

function capped(text: string): string {
  return text.length <= MAX_CHARS ? text : `${text.slice(0, MAX_CHARS)}${NEWLINE}[truncated: the section is longer than ${MAX_CHARS} characters]`;
}

export function readDoc(root: string, path: string, section?: string): ToolResult {
  const file = safeDocument(root, path);

  if (file === undefined) {
    return {
      text: `No document "${path}" in docs/. Use a path that search_docs gives.`,
      isError: true,
    };
  }
  const text = read(file);

  if (section === undefined || section.trim() === '') {
    return {
      text: tableOfContents(path, text),
      isError: false,
    };
  }
  const title = findSection(headings(text), section);

  if (title === undefined) {
    return {
      text: `No section "${section}" in ${path}.${NEWLINE}${tableOfContents(path, text)}`,
      isError: true,
    };
  }
  const body = sectionBody(text, title);
  const sources = title === 'Sources' ? [] : citedSources(text, body);

  return {
    text: capped([
      body,
      ...sources.length === 0 ? [] : [
        '',
        'Sources:',
        ...sources,
      ],
    ].join(NEWLINE)),
    isError: false,
  };
}

function callTool(root: string, params: Json): ToolResult | undefined {
  const args = typeof params.arguments === 'object' && params.arguments !== null ? params.arguments as Json : {};
  const text = (name: string): string | undefined => typeof args[name] === 'string' ? args[name] : undefined;

  if (params.name === 'search_docs') {
    return searchDocs(root, text('query') ?? '');
  }
  if (params.name === 'read_doc') {
    return readDoc(root, text('path') ?? '', text('section'));
  }
  return undefined;
}

function requestVersion(params: Json | undefined): string | undefined {
  const meta = params?._meta;

  if (typeof meta !== 'object' || meta === null) {
    return undefined;
  }
  const version = (meta as Json)[VERSION_KEY];

  return typeof version === 'string' ? version : undefined;
}

function reply(id: Request['id'], result: Json): Json {
  return {
    jsonrpc: '2.0',
    id,
    result,
  };
}

function failure(id: Request['id'], code: number, message: string, data?: Json): Json {
  return {
    jsonrpc: '2.0',
    id,
    error: data === undefined ? {
      code,
      message,
    } : {
      code,
      message,
      data,
    },
  };
}

function initialize(request: Request): Json {
  const requested = request.params?.protocolVersion;
  const version = typeof requested === 'string' && LEGACY_VERSIONS.includes(requested) ? requested : LEGACY_VERSIONS[0];

  return reply(request.id, {
    protocolVersion: version,
    capabilities: { tools: {} },
    serverInfo: SERVER_INFO,
    instructions: INSTRUCTIONS,
  });
}

function discover(request: Request): Json {
  return reply(request.id, {
    resultType: 'complete',
    supportedVersions: [
      MODERN_VERSION,
      ...LEGACY_VERSIONS,
    ],
    capabilities: { tools: {} },
    _meta: { 'io.modelcontextprotocol/serverInfo': SERVER_INFO },
    instructions: INSTRUCTIONS,
  });
}

function toolCall(root: string, request: Request, modern: boolean): Json {
  const result = callTool(root, request.params ?? {});

  if (result === undefined) {
    return failure(request.id, -32602, `Unknown tool: ${String(request.params?.name)}`);
  }
  return reply(request.id, {
    ...modern ? { resultType: 'complete' } : {},
    content: [
      {
        type: 'text',
        text: result.text,
      },
    ],
    isError: result.isError,
  });
}

function operation(root: string, request: Request, modern: boolean): Json {
  switch (request.method) {
    case 'ping': {
      return reply(request.id, {});
    }
    case 'tools/list': {
      return reply(request.id, {
        ...modern ? { resultType: 'complete' } : {},
        tools: TOOLS,
      });
    }
    case 'tools/call': {
      return toolCall(root, request, modern);
    }
    default: {
      return failure(request.id, -32601, `Method not found: ${String(request.method)}`);
    }
  }
}

// Answers one request, or returns undefined for a notification.
export function handle(root: string, request: Request): Json | undefined {
  if (request.id === undefined || request.id === null) {
    return undefined;
  }
  if (request.method === 'initialize') {
    return initialize(request);
  }
  const version = requestVersion(request.params);

  if (version !== undefined && version !== MODERN_VERSION) {
    return failure(request.id, -32022, 'Unsupported protocol version', {
      supported: [
        MODERN_VERSION,
        ...LEGACY_VERSIONS,
      ],
      requested: version,
    });
  }
  if (request.method === 'server/discover') {
    return discover(request);
  }
  return operation(root, request, version !== undefined);
}

export function handleLine(root: string, line: string): Json | undefined {
  if (line.trim() === '') {
    return undefined;
  }
  try {
    const request = JSON.parse(line) as unknown;

    if (typeof request !== 'object' || request === null || Array.isArray(request)) {
      return failure(null, -32600, 'Invalid request');
    }
    return handle(root, request as Request);
  } catch {
    return failure(null, -32700, 'Parse error');
  }
}

function main(): void {
  const { values } = parseArgs({ options: { root: { type: 'string' } } });
  const root = resolveDocsRoot(resolve(values.root ?? process.cwd()));
  const lines = createInterface({ input: process.stdin });

  process.stderr.write(`docs server: reading ${join(root, 'docs')}${NEWLINE}`);
  lines.on('line', (line) => {
    const answer = handleLine(root, line);

    if (answer !== undefined) {
      process.stdout.write(`${JSON.stringify(answer)}${NEWLINE}`);
    }
  });
}

if (process.argv[1] !== undefined && realpathSync(process.argv[1]) === realpathSync(fileURLToPath(import.meta.url))) {
  main();
}

# Knowledge tools

- Call `query_knowledge_files` with the question of the user before you answer.
- If it finds nothing, call it again with one plain keyword. Then try a second keyword.
- To read a whole document, call `view_knowledge_file` with the file id of a result.
- Name each document you used, by its title, on a last line that starts with `Sources:`.

=====BASE-EXPORT-1-eccf69c80ed6===== FILE 1/1 a6dc78df7dee2837bb07686a320a1aff9e1e53081223feae5697ff104082ab1d RAW adapters/open-webui/push.ts
// Pushes the chat profile to Open WebUI through its REST API: knowledge bases, skills and models,
// each shared for reading as OPEN_WEBUI_SHARE says. Written for Open WebUI 0.13.3.
// Run from the base root, after the export wrote chat/ and adapters/open-webui/manifest.json:
//   node adapters/open-webui/push.ts [--dry-run]
// Node 22.18 or later. No build step. No npm package.
//
// Environment:
//   OPEN_WEBUI_URL         Base address of the instance.
//   OPEN_WEBUI_TOKEN       API key or session token. Never written to a file.
//   OPEN_WEBUI_BASE_MODEL  Id of the base model every custom model wraps.
//   OPEN_WEBUI_SHARE       "private", "public" or "group:<group name>". Default: private.
//   OPEN_WEBUI_MODE        "native" or "legacy". Default: the mode of the manifest.
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import {
  existsSync,
  readdirSync,
  readFileSync,
  statSync,
  writeFileSync,
} from 'node:fs';
import {
  basename,
  dirname,
  join,
  relative,
} from 'node:path';
import { pathToFileURL } from 'node:url';

type Mode = 'native' | 'legacy';

interface Grant {
  principal_type: 'user' | 'group';
  principal_id: string;
  permission: 'read' | 'write';
}

interface KnowledgeEntry {
  key: string;
  name: string;
  description: string;
  dir: string;
}

interface SkillEntry {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  description: string;
  dir: string;
}

interface ModelEntry {
  id: string;
  name: string;
  description: string;
  prompt: string;
  knowledge: string[];
  skills: string[];
}

interface Manifest {
  mode: Mode;
  toolsBlock: string;
  knowledge: KnowledgeEntry[];
  skills: SkillEntry[];
  models: ModelEntry[];
}

interface State {
  knowledge: Record<string, string>;
}

interface LocalFile {
  data: Buffer;
  filename: string;
  path: string;
  checksum: string;
  size: number;
}

interface SyncDiff {
  added: {
    filename: string;
    path: string;
  }[];
  modified: {
    filename: string;
    path: string;
    stale_file_id: string;
  }[];
  deleted: { file_id: string }[];
  mkdir: string[];
  rmdir: string[];
  directory_map: Record<string, string>;
}

export interface PushConfig {
  url: string;
  token: string;
  baseModel: string;
  share: string;
  mode?: Mode;
  root: string;
  dryRun: boolean;
  log: (line: string) => void;
  pollMs?: number;
}

type Call = (method: string, path: string, body?: unknown, form?: FormData) => Promise<unknown>;

class ApiError extends Error {
  readonly status: number;

  constructor(message: string, status: number) {
    super(message);
    this.status = status;
  }
}

const OFF_TOOLS = [
  'time',
  'user_input',
  'memory',
  'chats',
  'notes',
  'files',
  'channels',
  'notifications',
  'web_search',
  'image_generation',
  'code_interpreter',
  'tasks',
  'automations',
  'calendar',
  'subagents',
  'skills',
  'tools',
];

function record(value: unknown): Record<string, unknown> {
  return typeof value === 'object' && value !== null ? Object.fromEntries(Object.entries(value)) : {};
}

function text(value: unknown): string {
  return typeof value === 'string' ? value : '';
}

function createCall(config: PushConfig): Call {
  const base = config.url.replace(/\/+$/, '');

  return async (method, path, body, form) => {
    if (config.dryRun && method !== 'GET') {
      config.log(`dry-run ${method} ${path}`);
      return {};
    }
    const headers: Record<string, string> = { Authorization: `Bearer ${config.token}` };

    if (form === undefined && body !== undefined) {
      headers['Content-Type'] = 'application/json';
    }
    const response = await fetch(base + path, {
      method,
      headers,
      body: form ?? (body === undefined ? undefined : JSON.stringify(body)),
    });
    const answer = await response.text();

    if (!response.ok) {
      throw new ApiError(`${method} ${path} -> ${String(response.status)} ${answer.slice(0, 300)}`, response.status);
    }
    return answer === '' ? null : JSON.parse(answer) as unknown;
  };
}

function walk(folder: string): string[] {
  return readdirSync(folder)
    .toSorted()
    .filter((entry) => !entry.startsWith('.'))
    .flatMap((entry) => {
      const full = join(folder, entry);

      return statSync(full).isDirectory() ? walk(full) : [full];
    });
}

function posixRelative(from: string, to: string): string {
  return relative(from, to)
    .split('\\')
    .join('/');
}

async function resolveGrants(call: Call, share: string): Promise<Grant[]> {
  if (share === 'private') {
    return [];
  }
  if (share === 'public') {
    return [
      {
        principal_type: 'user',
        principal_id: '*',
        permission: 'read',
      },
    ];
  }
  if (!share.startsWith('group:')) {
    throw new Error(`OPEN_WEBUI_SHARE must be private, public or group:<name>, got ${share}`);
  }
  const wanted = share.slice('group:'.length);
  const groups = await call('GET', '/api/v1/groups/');
  const group = (Array.isArray(groups) ? groups : [])
    .map(record)
    .find((item) => item['name'] === wanted);

  if (group === undefined) {
    throw new Error(`Group not found: ${wanted}`);
  }
  return [
    {
      principal_type: 'group',
      principal_id: text(group['id']),
      permission: 'read',
    },
  ];
}

async function existingSkillVersion(call: Call, id: string): Promise<string> {
  try {
    return text(record(await call('GET', `/api/v1/skills/id/${id}`))['version_id']);
  } catch (error) {
    if (error instanceof ApiError && error.status === 404) {
      return '';
    }
    throw error;
  }
}

async function pushSkill(call: Call, config: PushConfig, skill: SkillEntry, grants: Grant[]): Promise<void> {
  const folder = join(config.root, skill.dir);
  const files = walk(folder).map((file) => {
    return {
      path: posixRelative(folder, file),
      content: readFileSync(file, 'utf8'),
    };
  });
  const form = {
    id: skill.id,
    name: skill.name,
    description: skill.description,
    files,
    meta: { tags: [] },
    is_active: true,
  };
  const version = await existingSkillVersion(call, skill.id);

  if (version === '') {
    await call('POST', '/api/v1/skills/create', {
      ...form,
      access_grants: grants,
    });
  } else {
    await call('POST', `/api/v1/skills/id/${skill.id}/update`, {
      ...form,
      expected_version_id: version,
    });
  }
  await call('POST', `/api/v1/skills/id/${skill.id}/access/update`, { access_grants: grants });
  config.log(`skill ${skill.id}: ${String(files.length)} file(s)`);
}

function localFiles(folder: string): LocalFile[] {
  return walk(folder).map((file) => {
    const data = readFileSync(file);
    const path = dirname(posixRelative(folder, file));

    return {
      data,
      filename: basename(file),
      path: path === '.' ? '' : path,
      checksum: createHash('sha256')
        .update(data)
        .digest('hex'),
      size: data.length,
    };
  });
}

async function knowledgeId(call: Call, config: PushConfig, entry: KnowledgeEntry, grants: Grant[], state: State): Promise<string> {
  const known = state.knowledge[entry.key] ?? '';

  if (known !== '') {
    try {
      await call('GET', `/api/v1/knowledge/${known}`);
      return known;
    } catch (error) {
      if (!(error instanceof ApiError) || error.status !== 404) {
        throw error;
      }
    }
  }
  const created = record(await call('POST', '/api/v1/knowledge/create', {
    name: entry.name,
    description: entry.description,
    access_grants: grants,
  }));
  const id = text(created['id']) || `dry-run:${entry.key}`;

  state.knowledge[entry.key] = id;
  return id;
}

async function syncDiff(call: Call, config: PushConfig, id: string, local: LocalFile[]): Promise<SyncDiff> {
  if (config.dryRun) {
    return {
      added: local,
      modified: [],
      deleted: [],
      mkdir: [],
      rmdir: [],
      directory_map: {},
    };
  }
  const manifest = local.map((file) => {
    return {
      filename: file.filename,
      path: file.path,
      checksum: file.checksum,
      size: file.size,
    };
  });

  return await call('POST', `/api/v1/knowledge/${id}/sync/diff`, { manifest }) as SyncDiff;
}

async function waitForFile(call: Call, config: PushConfig, fileId: string): Promise<void> {
  for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 300; attempt += 1) {
    const status = text(record(await call('GET', `/api/v1/files/${fileId}/process/status`))['status']);

    if (status === 'completed') {
      return;
    }
    if (status === 'failed') {
      throw new Error(`File processing failed: ${fileId}`);
    }
    await new Promise((resolve) => {
      setTimeout(resolve, config.pollMs ?? 1000);
    });
  }
  throw new Error(`File processing timed out: ${fileId}`);
}

async function makeDirectories(call: Call, id: string, paths: string[], directories: Record<string, string>): Promise<void> {
  for (const path of paths) {
    const parent = dirname(path) === '.' ? null : directories[dirname(path)] ?? null;
    const created = record(await call('POST', `/api/v1/knowledge/${id}/dirs/create`, {
      name: basename(path),
      parent_id: parent,
    }));

    directories[path] = text(created['id']) || `dry-run:${path}`;
  }
}

async function upload(call: Call, config: PushConfig, id: string, file: LocalFile, directories: Record<string, string>): Promise<void> {
  const metadata: Record<string, string> = {
    knowledge_id: id,
    file_hash: file.checksum,
  };

  if (file.path !== '') {
    metadata['directory_id'] = directories[file.path] ?? '';
  }
  const form = new FormData();

  form.append('file', new Blob([file.data], { type: 'text/markdown' }), file.filename);
  form.append('metadata', JSON.stringify(metadata));
  const uploaded = record(await call('POST', '/api/v1/files/?process=true&process_in_background=false', undefined, form));

  if (!config.dryRun) {
    await waitForFile(call, config, text(uploaded['id']));
  }
}

async function pushKnowledge(call: Call, config: PushConfig, entry: KnowledgeEntry, grants: Grant[], state: State): Promise<string> {
  const id = await knowledgeId(call, config, entry, grants, state);
  const local = localFiles(join(config.root, entry.dir));
  const diff = await syncDiff(call, config, id, local);
  const directories = { ...diff.directory_map };
  const changed = [
    ...diff.added,
    ...diff.modified,
  ];
  const stale = [
    ...diff.deleted.map((item) => item.file_id),
    ...diff.modified.map((item) => item.stale_file_id),
  ];

  await makeDirectories(call, id, diff.mkdir, directories);
  for (const item of changed) {
    const file = local.find((candidate) => candidate.filename === item.filename && candidate.path === item.path);

    if (file !== undefined) {
      await upload(call, config, id, file, directories);
    }
  }
  if (stale.length > 0 || diff.rmdir.length > 0) {
    await call('POST', `/api/v1/knowledge/${id}/sync/cleanup`, {
      file_ids: stale,
      dir_ids: diff.rmdir,
    });
  }
  await call('POST', `/api/v1/knowledge/${id}/access/update`, { access_grants: grants });
  config.log(`knowledge ${entry.key}: ${String(changed.length)} uploaded, ${String(stale.length)} removed, ${String(local.length)} local`);
  return id;
}

function systemPrompt(config: PushConfig, entry: ModelEntry, manifest: Manifest, mode: Mode): string {
  const prompt = readFileSync(join(config.root, entry.prompt), 'utf8')
    .replace(/^---\n[\s\S]*?\n---\n/, '')
    .trim();

  if (mode === 'native' && entry.knowledge.length > 0) {
    // The block is a document of its own, with a title; in the prompt it becomes one more section.
    const tools = readFileSync(join(config.root, manifest.toolsBlock), 'utf8')
      .trim()
      .replace(/^# /, '## ');

    return `${prompt}\n\n${tools}`;
  }
  return prompt;
}

interface ModelContext {
  config: PushConfig;
  manifest: Manifest;
  mode: Mode;
  ids: Record<string, string>;
  grants: Grant[];
}

function modelKnowledge(context: ModelContext, entry: ModelEntry): Record<string, string>[] {
  return entry.knowledge.map((key) => {
    const source = context.manifest.knowledge.find((item) => item.key === key);
    const id = context.ids[key];

    if (source === undefined || id === undefined) {
      throw new Error(`Unknown knowledge ${key} in model ${entry.id}`);
    }
    return {
      id,
      name: source.name,
      description: source.description,
      type: 'collection',
    };
  });
}

// Every built-in tool is off, except the knowledge tools in native mode for a model with knowledge.
function capabilities(usesKnowledgeTools: boolean): Record<string, unknown> {
  return {
    capabilities: {
      vision: false,
      file_upload: true,
      file_context: true,
      web_search: false,
      image_generation: false,
      code_interpreter: false,
      terminal: false,
      citations: true,
      status_updates: true,
      memory: false,
      builtin_tools: usesKnowledgeTools,
    },
    builtinTools: Object.fromEntries([
      ['knowledge', usesKnowledgeTools],
      ...OFF_TOOLS.map((key) => [
        key,
        false,
      ]),
    ]),
  };
}

export function buildModel(context: ModelContext, entry: ModelEntry): Record<string, unknown> {
  return {
    id: entry.id,
    name: entry.name,
    base_model_id: context.config.baseModel,
    is_active: true,
    params: {
      system: systemPrompt(context.config, entry, context.manifest, context.mode),
      function_calling: context.mode,
    },
    meta: {
      description: entry.description,
      ...capabilities(context.mode === 'native' && entry.knowledge.length > 0),
      knowledge: modelKnowledge(context, entry),
      skillIds: entry.skills,
      toolIds: [],
      defaultFeatureIds: [],
    },
    access_grants: context.grants,
  };
}

async function checkInstance(call: Call, config: PushConfig, mode: Mode): Promise<void> {
  const version = text(record(await call('GET', '/api/version'))['version']);

  config.log(`Open WebUI ${version}, mode ${mode}, share ${config.share}${config.dryRun ? ', dry run' : ''}`);
  const listed = record(await call('GET', '/api/models'))['data'];
  const ids = (Array.isArray(listed) ? listed : []).map((model) => text(record(model)['id']));

  if (!ids.includes(config.baseModel)) {
    throw new Error('Base model not found. Check OPEN_WEBUI_BASE_MODEL.');
  }
}

export async function push(config: PushConfig): Promise<void> {
  const adapter = join(config.root, 'adapters', 'open-webui');
  const stateFile = join(adapter, 'state.local.json');
  const manifest = JSON.parse(readFileSync(join(adapter, 'manifest.json'), 'utf8')) as Manifest;
  const mode = config.mode ?? manifest.mode;
  const call = createCall(config);
  const state = existsSync(stateFile) ? JSON.parse(readFileSync(stateFile, 'utf8')) as State : { knowledge: {} };

  await checkInstance(call, config, mode);
  const grants = await resolveGrants(call, config.share);

  for (const skill of manifest.skills) {
    await pushSkill(call, config, skill, grants);
  }
  const ids: Record<string, string> = {};

  for (const entry of manifest.knowledge) {
    ids[entry.key] = await pushKnowledge(call, config, entry, grants, state);
  }
  if (!config.dryRun) {
    writeFileSync(stateFile, `${JSON.stringify(state, null, 2)}\n`);
  }
  const context = {
    config,
    manifest,
    mode,
    ids,
    grants,
  };
  const models = manifest.models.map((entry) => buildModel(context, entry));

  await call('POST', '/api/v1/models/import', { models });
  for (const model of models) {
    await call('POST', '/api/v1/models/model/access/update', {
      id: model['id'],
      access_grants: grants,
    });
    config.log(`model ${text(model['id'])}`);
  }
  config.log('Done.');
}

function environment(name: string, fallback?: string): string {
  const value = process.env[name] ?? fallback ?? '';

  if (value === '') {
    throw new Error(`Missing environment variable ${name}`);
  }
  return value;
}

function modeFromEnvironment(): Mode | undefined {
  const value = process.env['OPEN_WEBUI_MODE'] ?? '';

  if (value === '') {
    return undefined;
  }
  if (value !== 'native' && value !== 'legacy') {
    throw new Error('OPEN_WEBUI_MODE must be native or legacy');
  }
  return value;
}

async function main(): Promise<void> {
  await push({
    url: environment('OPEN_WEBUI_URL'),
    token: environment('OPEN_WEBUI_TOKEN'),
    baseModel: environment('OPEN_WEBUI_BASE_MODEL'),
    share: environment('OPEN_WEBUI_SHARE', 'private'),
    mode: modeFromEnvironment(),
    root: process.cwd(),
    dryRun: process.argv.includes('--dry-run'),
    log: (line) => {
      process.stdout.write(`${line}\n`);
    },
  });
}

if (process.argv[1] !== undefined && import.meta.url === pathToFileURL(process.argv[1]).href) {
  main().catch((error: unknown) => {
    process.stderr.write(`${error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error)}\n`);
    process.exitCode = 1;
  });
}
